- **Missing-HTTP-timeout hang** — external-dependency HTTP call with no per-request read deadline wedges all workers indefinitely when an upstream gateway stalls a connection (zero bytes, ESTAB held for hours). Backends may be healthy; the bug is the missing client timeout. Deterministic check: every HTTP client against an external dependency sets `http.Client{Timeout}` or per-call `context.WithTimeout` + bounded retry. Ref: engram-go#1107.

---

### E. Lease/Reconciler Failure Modes

The in-progress orphan saga (engram-go#1108): an issue labeled `in-progress` with no live lease is invisible to the poll loop and to `lease_reclaimable()`. The active expiry-sweeper (codex-reconciler.sh) closes this gap by independently querying for all `in-progress` issues and reconciling against the lease store.

| ID | Name | Symptom | Fix |
|----|------|---------|-----|
| FM-135 | stale-lease-wrong-owner (FM-REC-01) | Issue labeled `in-progress` + lease exists but expired + different owner | Reconciler detects via `decide()` → `STALE_LEASE_WRONG_OWNER` → re-queue |
| FM-136 | reconciler-ABA (FM-REC-02) | Second reconciler sees same lease generation, re-queues already-recovered issue | Idempotency guard: CAS on lease generation before acting |
| FM-137 | strike-bounds-missing (FM-REC-03) | Unbounded recovery strikes on persistently failing issue burn loops forever | Self-quarantine after `CODEX_RECONCILER_MAX_STRIKES` (default 3) |
| FM-138 | 403-silent-release (FM-REC-04) | 403 on label write silently releases a live lease, orphaning the work | `requeue_issue()` fails-loud on non-zero gh exit, NEVER releases on 403 |
| FM-139 | duplicate-host-identity (FM-REC-05) | Two processes with same POLLER_ID both claim; second double-executes | Lease identity must carry process generation; `decide()` detects `DUAL_CLAIM` → `freeze_issue()` |
| FM-140 | clock-skew (FM-REC-06) | Clock jump ahead causes mass premature lease expiry and mass re-queue | Lease expiry should have a grace window; noted for future hardening (not implemented) |
| FM-141 | reconciler-crash-mid-heal (FM-REC-07) | Crash between re-queue label write and strike decrement; rerun duplicates | Idempotent rerun: requeue is always retried; strike counter only increments |
| FM-142 | label-projection-staleness (FM-REC-08) | Dead projector means in-progress label stale while lease store is correct | Reconciler heartbeat file (`CODEX_RECONCILER_HEARTBEAT_FILE`) must alarm if stale |
| FM-143 | toctou-reap-deletes-renewed-lease (FM-REC-09) | Foreign poller RENEWS its lease between `decide()` reading it as expired and `reap_stale_foreign_lease()` deleting it; reap removes a now-LIVE lease → issue re-queued while foreign host still runs it → DUAL-EXECUTE | Reap re-reads `expires_at`+`owner` under `flock` immediately before `rm`; SKIPs delete if now live (`now < expires_at`) or owner changed |
| FM-144 | silent-unreadable-store-mass-orphan (FM-REC-10) | Lease dir unreadable/unwritable (e.g. systemd `ProtectHome=read-only` with default `CODEX_POLL_LEASE_DIR=${HOME}/.cache/...` not overridden to a ReadWritePath); reconciler scans empty store → classifies EVERY in-progress issue as `ORPHAN_NO_LEASE` → mass force-requeue of all live work | `assert_lease_store_usable()` fails CLOSED at startup: probes read+write, logs FATAL and EXITs non-zero BEFORE any `decide()`/requeue; systemd unit pins lease dir under `ReadWritePaths` |
| FM-145 | label-reasserted-on-closed-issue (FM-REC-11) | `reconcile_label_missing()` second pass re-asserts `in-progress` on a completed+CLOSED issue whose lease hasn't expired (closed issues absent from `--state open` first pass) every sweep until TTL → misleading label + API-hammer loop | Verify issue is OPEN (`gh issue view --json state`) before `upsert_label`; skip if closed/merged or state unconfirmable |
| FM-98 | Claude CLI session expiry | `claude --print` exits with status 1 after ~5h for runs using `--generation-model sonnet` with no error message logged in run.log; checkpoint recovers on re-run (engram-go LME session, 2026-06-20). | For long-running generation tasks, expect a retry pass after ~4h. Mitigation: break into smaller batches or use non-Claude runners for tasks exceeding the 5h window. Observe: all Sonnet runs >4h this session hit exit-1 mid-run. |
| FM-99 | LME binary stale on hypothesis flag | `longmemeval.real` not rebuilt after a new flag was added to `cmd/longmemeval/main.go` (e.g. `--preference-enumerate`) — the binary silently doesn't recognize the flag, run fails with "flag provided but not defined" (engram-go LME session, 2026-06-20). | Always run `cd ~/projects/engram-go && go build -o longmemeval.real ./cmd/longmemeval/` before launching any hypothesis run. This is a prerequisite to every hypothesis invocation, not a per-fix step. |
| FM-100 | Deploy / Verify Discipline — cross-consumer fix-drift | A fix (delivery-mode / artifact-gate downgrade to `comment_only`) was applied to the Go `fleet-attach` consumer but not mirrored to the sibling bash `codex-attach` consumer — the same bug persisted on the unpatched path, silently. Root cause: sibling consumers share a contract but not a code path; a patch to one does not propagate to the other. (2026-06-19, fleet-dispatch) | For every fleet-dispatch protocol fix, enumerate ALL consumers before closing the issue and verify each one is patched. Cross-consumer parity test: enqueue an item on the lane of each consumer and assert terminal outcome matches. Detection: diff the claim/report logic across sibling consumers after any protocol change. | Judgment |
| FM-101 | Fail-Loud — consumer alive but non-executing | Attach service shows `active`/polling, zero auth errors, but every claimed item fails on a local precondition (dirty working tree / missing branch) — the service passes all liveness and status checks while doing zero real work. Because liveness probes only verify the process, not the work-execution path, the failure is invisible until a round-trip canary times out. (2026-06-19, codex-attach.service) | A process/liveness check is NOT a work-execution check. Complement all consumer liveness probes with a scheduled real round-trip canary that enqueues a minimal item and asserts a terminal `done` within a bounded window. A consumer that claims but never completes is indistinguishable from a healthy consumer unless the deliverable is verified. Cross-ref FM-87 (terminal status ≠ deliverable success). | Judgment |
| FM-102 | Fail-Loud — capability string unvalidated server-side | Fleet-dispatch capability strings are free-form text gated only by token Produces/Consumes lists. A typo (`impl-grook` instead of `impl-grok`) or a newly-registered capability that no consumer declares is silently never claimed: the item sits in `queued` indefinitely with no error surfaced to the producer. Detection requires polling the item status manually. (2026-06-19) | Server must validate capability at enqueue time against a registry of known capabilities and return a 422 with an explicit error if unknown. Until server-side validation exists: after every enqueue, assert the item is claimed within the expected window (claim canary). Detection check: `enqueue → poll → assert state != queued after N seconds`. | 🤖 |
| FM-103 | Install / Config Layout — ATTACH_TOKENS atomic-blob hazard | `ATTACH_TOKENS` is a single JSON object keyed by live bearer hex strings. Any edit (add/remove/rotate a token) requires a full read-merge-write cycle. A JSON syntax error during that cycle (malformed merge, trailing comma, copy-paste mistake) corrupts the entire map and returns 401 to ALL consumers simultaneously — the fleet closes hard with no partial fallback. One bad token rotation can silently kill the entire dispatch bus. (2026-06-19, fleet-dispatch) | Treat `ATTACH_TOKENS` as crown-jewel config: (1) always read the current value, apply a structured merge tool (e.g. `jq` merge), and JSON-validate (`jq . >/dev/null`) before writing; (2) retain the previous value in a rollback variable for the duration of the rotation window; (3) after write, verify the new token works on a canary request before the old token is invalidated; (4) enforce supervised edits only — no raw `kubectl edit`. Cross-ref FM-86 (ESO-managed secret write path). | Judgment |
| FM-104 | Deploy / Verify Discipline — stale queue item missing expected_delivery hits artifact gate | Items enqueued before `expected_delivery` was a required field (or enqueued via a producer that omitted it) hit the fleet-dispatch artifact gate on `/report` with HTTP 422, because the server requires `expected_delivery` to determine terminal disposition. The item was `comment_only`-intended but the gate cannot confirm that without the field — it fails closed. (2026-06-19, fleet-dispatch) | `expected_delivery` must be stored by the server at enqueue time as a first-class field (not derived at report time). Verify: `SELECT expected_delivery FROM items WHERE id=<id>` immediately after enqueue returns non-null. Pre-merge gate: any item without `expected_delivery` at enqueue must be rejected 422 at enqueue (not at report). | 🤖 |
| FM-105 | Deploy / Verify Discipline — acting on unverified diagnosis (diagnosis drift) | First-pass diagnoses were wrong in both directions this session: optimistically (phantom blockers declared that did not exist) and pessimistically (mis-rooted root cause that led to a fix for the wrong thing). In both cases, a fix was built and deployed before the diagnosis was confirmed with a live probe. Cross-ref FM-70 (trust calibration: observation vs. conclusion). (2026-06-19) | Before building any fix: (1) confirm root cause with a live probe — read the actual code path, not the inferred one; (2) distinguish observations (log line, return code) from conclusions (this is why); (3) after applying a fix, reproduce the original symptom to confirm it is gone before declaring done. Minimum bar: a one-liner that demonstrates the bug before the fix and does not demonstrate it after. | Judgment |
| FM-106 | Deploy / Verify Discipline — incident-report target ambiguity | A reported crisis ("disk full") was about a different target (local install on the user's machine) than assumed (container/cluster). Diagnosis proceeded on the wrong target for several turns before the scope was clarified. (2026-06-19) | Before diagnosing any reported failure: ask or verify the exact host/container/namespace that is affected. A one-line scope confirmation ("this error is on host X, not container Y") costs nothing; a mis-targeted diagnosis burns cycles and may apply a harmful fix to the wrong system. Confirm: `hostname`, `kubectl config current-context`, or `docker inspect <container>` as appropriate to the report. | Judgment |
| FM-107 | Deploy / Verify Discipline — consult-lane consumer race / non-deterministic routing | Codex and Hermes both consume the `consult` capability. Codex claims consult items and either 422s (its coordinator token does not produce/consult-emit correctly) or runs `gh issue view placeholder/placeholder#0` (fleet-consult.sh uses a placeholder repo) and dead-letters. The fence/retry mechanism eventually recovers via Hermes, but routing is non-deterministic and recovery time is unbounded. (fleet-dispatch, 2026-06-20) | A capability must have exactly ONE intended consumer; remove `consult` from the Codex token Consumes[] so only Hermes claims it. Detection: after enqueue, assert `lease_holder` matches the expected consumer's node (not just that the item reached `done`). Pre-deploy gate: enumerate all tokens' Consumes[] lists and flag any capability claimed by more than one consumer class. | Judgment |
| FM-108 | Fail-Loud — Codex CLI implements on comment-intended review/feedback prompt → push-blocked dead-letter | A `review`-capability item whose prompt asks for plan feedback (not a code artifact) causes the Codex CLI to parse implementation cues, create a local `agent/codex/*` branch with commits, then fail because `git push` is allow-list-blocked → dead-letter with a preserved unpushed worktree. The failure is not surfaced until the dead-letter reason is inspected manually. (fleet-dispatch, 2026-06-20) | Route plan-feedback through a `comment_only` capability (not artifact-expecting `review`); enforce a plan-only contract in codex-poll when feedback is intended; phrase review/feedback prompts to avoid deliverable/implementation cues. Detection: dead-letter reason mentions "preserved unpushed work" or a branch with unpushed commits. Pre-enqueue gate: capability=`review` items must specify a code artifact as the deliverable; plan-feedback items use `comment_only`. | Judgment |
| FM-109 | Fail-Loud | POST /item/{id}/cancel returns 409 when the item is in dead-letter state. Dead-letter items cannot be cleaned up via the standard cancel path, leaving them in the queue and any preserved consumer worktrees unreclaimed. The cancel endpoint should be idempotent across all terminal states (done, dead-letter, cancelled). (fleet-dispatch, 2026-06-20) | Cancel endpoint must be idempotent across all terminal states (done, dead-letter, cancelled); verify that POST /item/{id}/cancel returns 204 for any terminal state, not 409. Pre-deploy gate: integration test that asserts cancel on a dead-letter item succeeds. | Judgment |
| FM-110 | Fail-Loud | A # pragma: no cover live binding (live_run_py) shipped a NameError (stripped 'import pathlib') because a formatter PostToolUse hook removed the import between two edits while a shadowing inner import still existed; all unit tests injected a fake run_py so the real binding was never executed and 121 tests passed green. | Every '# pragma: no cover' live binding has at least one real (non-faked) smoke test that actually invokes it; and after editing an import block in a formatter-hooked repo, grep-confirm the import survived the hook. | Judgment |
| FM-111 | Fail-Loud | In factvault cmd/worker_rss.go, --tenant flag was registered in cobra but the value was never read into the workerConfig struct, so it was silently dropped. | Verify each cobra flag has a corresponding struct field assignment and is exercised in at least one integration test | Judgment |
| FM-112 | Portability | In factvault cmd/api.go, the --addr flag had no FACTVAULT_API_ADDR env-var fallback, forcing operators to modify command-line invocations in container deployments instead of setting an env var. | Every CLI --addr/--host/--port flag must have a corresponding env-var fallback registered via viper or equivalent | Judgment |
| FM-113 | Agent Coordination — Batch-dispatch of semi-related tasks produces N conflicting PRs | Coordinator dispatches N individually-queued Codex issues for a batch of tasks in the same repo without grouping by file-area ownership. Codex picks them all up in parallel, opens N draft PRs simultaneously, and they conflict each other on shared files (e.g., docker-compose.yml, go flags files, docs). Root cause: parallel-safe check only catches exact file-path overlap in plan files, not "same file-area" overlap. A batch of OOBE fixes touching cmd/, deploy/, docs/ in one repo is effectively one logical unit dispatched as N separate units. Cost: N round-trips to Codex for rebases; wasted CI cycles; merge churn. Fix: (1) GROUP by file-area — bundle tasks that span the same repo+file-area into 1–3 issues by area (cmd/ fixes → one issue, deploy/ fixes → one issue, docs fixes → one issue); (2) if keeping separate issues, enforce strict sequence — remove parallel-safe from all but the first; each must merge before the next is dispatched; (3) never mark issues parallel-safe within the same repo batch unless file sets are truly disjoint (different packages, different service boundaries). Introduced: 2026-06-22 — factvault OOBE batch (issues #210–#217, 8 issues → 8 simultaneous PRs → 5 conflicts on first merge pass). | Judgment |
| FM-114 | Agent Coordination / Dispatch — fleet-enqueue with short repo name → malformed ref → silent dead-letter | Enqueuing a fleet-dispatch item with bare repo name (e.g., `--repo factvault`) instead of full slug (`--repo petersimmons1972/factvault`). The enqueue command SUCCEEDS and returns an enqueue ID, giving false confidence. The codex-attach consumer requires refs in `[HOST/]OWNER/REPO` format; a bare name produces a malformed ref. Consumer cannot resolve it, item never claims, queue reaper dead-letters it within seconds. GitHub issue label stays `agent/codex/queued` (cosmetic), masking the failure. Compounding: discovered alongside a dead consumer (codex-agent container down ~10h); consumer downtime hid the slug bug because items dead-lettered for "no consumer" reasons instead of malformed ref. When consumer restarted, items STILL failed with "expected [HOST/]OWNER/REPO format, got factvault". Cost: hours of apparent "queued but idle" state; two false-start re-enqueue rounds. Introduced: 2026-06-23 — factvault #210/#211/#213/#216/#217 re-enqueue after OOBE batch rebases. | (1) ALWAYS use full `OWNER/REPO` slug to fleet-enqueue.sh (`petersimmons1972/factvault`), never bare repo name. (2) Enhance fleet-enqueue.sh to validate --repo arg matches `^[^/]+/[^/]+$` (or `[HOST/]OWNER/REPO`) and reject bare names at enqueue time (file enhancement on fleet-dispatch). (3) When items show `queued` on GitHub label but never claim, check actual queue item state via fleet-dispatch /items API — the label lies; the queue state is truth. | Judgment |
| FM-115 | Observability / Agent Coordination — Silent consumer death, no liveness/staleness alerting on dispatch pipeline | The codex-attach consumer (Docker container codex-agent) died and stayed dead ~10 hours. Nothing alerted. The queue kept accepting enqueues; ~50 items dead-lettered silently, and GitHub issue labels stayed cosmetically `agent/codex/queued`. Discovered only by manual inspection after PRs sat idle. Root cause: no automated detection for "consumer alive but not consuming" or "consumer dead." Three missing detection layers: (1) Consumer liveness — no per-capability last-claim heartbeat + staleness alert; (2) Queue health — no alert on items queued > N min unclaimed, no dead-letter-rate alert (50 dead-letters accumulated with zero notification); (3) End-to-end canary — no scheduled synthetic item proving the pipeline completes work. Compounding: GitHub labels are cosmetic and lie (stay `queued`); the fleet-dispatch /items API is the only truth source for actual queue state. Cost: ~10h pipeline outage undetected; multiple false-start re-enqueues; manual diagnosis. Observed: 2026-06-23 — codex-agent container dead during factvault OOBE rebase cycle, items dead-lettered in silence. | (1) Per-capability liveness: alert when a capability has queued items but no consumer claim/heartbeat in 15 min. (2) Queue-health alerts: item-age threshold + dead-letter-rate spike alert. (3) Scheduled end-to-end canary (enqueue trivial no-op every ~10 min; alert if not completed within SLA) — catches ALL pipeline failure causes regardless of root cause. (4) Container-level: Docker healthcheck that probes the attach-loop liveness (not just process-exists) + autoheal sidecar for self-recovery; keep canary alerting so silent auto-restarts don't mask deeper faults. Tracked: fleet-dispatch#65 (canary + LAST_SUCCESS staleness) covers layers 1+3; fleet-dispatch#67 (structured harness logging) feeds the alerting. Prioritize #65 to p1 after this incident. | Judgment |
| FM-116 | K8s Networking — Default-Deny Egress | substack-scheduler silently down ~11 days; pod showed 1/1 Ready/Running but SSH :22 egress blocked; default-deny egress policy required explicit NetworkPolicy allow rule that was never added | Default-deny egress: pod Ready ≠ functional; verify logs for actual behavior, not pod status | Judgment |
| FM-117 | GitOps Declarative Drift | substack-scheduler NetworkPolicy (allow-github-egress / allow-substack-egress) and GHCR pull Secret were applied/edited live via kubectl; without persisting them into scheduler/k8s source manifests (and Infisical for the secret), they reverted on the next reconcile or rebuild | ANY live cluster fix MUST be written into the source manifests at the GitOps-watched path and merged to the tracked branch before it is considered done. Before applying live, check for GitOps reconciliation (ArgoCD/Flux) + ESO; edit SOURCE (repo/Infisical), not live. Verify the fix is present on origin/<branch> via `git show origin/<branch>:<path>`, never working tree alone. | Judgment |
| FM-119 | Fail-Loud / Fleet-Dispatch — Fleet consumer silently not claiming: wrong-token default + gate-reset-on-deploy, no alerting | Queued items for capability `impl-grok` sat indefinitely with `retries=0`, `lease_holder:""`. Consumer container healthy (uptime + healthcheck pass), fleet-dispatch server healthy, no error surfaced anywhere. Compound root causes: (1) `deploy-grok-machine.sh:~51` defaults `FLEET_TOKEN_FILE` to `token-impl` (Codex token) instead of `token-impl-grok`; server returns `{empty:true}` on every `/claim` because that token's `consumes` set lacks the target capability — silently; (2) `deploy-grok-machine.sh:~127` always writes `GROK_ATTACH_ENABLED=0`, disabling claiming on every redeploy regardless of prior state; (3) no alert fires on "items queued with no lease for N minutes" or "consumer token `consumes` does not include its target capability." Burned a multi-step diagnostic cycle (poll → k8s health → host docker → token-sha comparison) before isolating the cause. (Bug: petersimmons1972/claude-codex#316) | (a) Deploy default must use the capability-specific token (`token-impl-grok`), not the generic `token-impl`; validate at startup. (b) Persist gate state across redeploys, or make ENABLED the default post-cutover. (c) Consumer must log LOUDLY at startup when its token's `consumes` does not include the capability it was deployed to serve. (d) Alert on queue items with `lease_holder:""` older than N minutes and on a consumer whose token `consumes` is empty for its intended capability. Detection: `docker logs <consumer> --tail=25` → "standing by (disabled mode)" or empty-claim loops; compare token sha256 vs intended capability token; `GET /item/<id>` for persistent empty `lease_holder`. | Judgment |
| FM-120 | Fail-Loud / Infra / Config-Drift — Running container/config drifts from manifest: hand-edits and deploy resets diverge live state from source-of-truth, neither reconciles | The live running container's effective config differs from what the declared manifest / deploy script / IaC source-of-truth would produce. Hand-fixes to the running host (sed-editing a live `.env`, `kubectl patch` on a secret) appear applied but are silently reverted on the next deploy or by ESO reconciliation; conversely, deploy scripts overwrite intended runtime state (e.g. resetting an enable gate to 0) on every run. "What's running" ≠ "what's declared," and nothing flags the gap. Root causes: (1) Deploy script writes authoritative values that overwrite operationally-critical runtime state on every redeploy (`deploy-grok-machine.sh:~127` forces `GROK_ATTACH_ENABLED=0`); (2) Operators hand-patch live state to work around it (sed on `.env`), creating drift the next deploy silently reverts; (3) ESO-managed k8s secrets (`fleet-dispatch-tokens`) have their source of truth in Infisical; direct `kubectl patch` looks applied but is reverted on next sync; (4) No drift detection comparing running effective config to the declared manifest / IaC source. Each layer (enable gate, consumer token, server-side `consumes` registration) gets patched at the LIVE container instead of at the source of truth, so it re-breaks on the next reconcile/deploy. Cost: this session — a hand-edited gate and a proposed k8s-secret patch would each have been reverted; compounded the FM-119 diagnostic chase. Related: FM-119, petersimmons1972/claude-codex#316. | After ANY deploy or ESO sync, diff the running container's effective env/config against (a) the deploy script's output and (b) the IaC source; re-verify runtime invariants (gate on, token identity, consumes set). Make every config change at the source of truth only — Infisical `ATTACH_TOKENS` for tokens/consumes (then force-sync + rollout-restart), the deploy script defaults / committed `.env` template for runtime flags — never hand-edit live state. Deploy scripts must PRESERVE operationally-critical state (don't hard-reset an enable gate). Add a reconcile/drift check (running vs declared) to the consumer healthcheck so divergence is loud, not silent. | Judgment |
| FM-122 | Deploy / Verify Discipline — live-deploy drift / running-ahead-of-manifest (extends FM-120) | A running container or host-installed artifact is ahead of its source-of-truth: a `cp`/hand-edit applied to a live container without the identical change committed to source + image; a deployment rolled to an image tag the repo manifest does not pin; a `kubectl apply` of a change not yet on `main`. On the next rebuild, restart, or GitOps reconcile the running state silently regresses — symptom appears hours or days later with no clear cause. Observed: fleet-dispatch overnight runs #76/#78 (in-container `git pull`+`cp` of codex-poll NFF+lint patches); any `kubectl set image` outside a PR cycle. | (a) Manifest-first: before or simultaneously with any deploy, commit the change to source AND update the manifest/image so rebuild reproduces running state exactly. (b) After any deploy, run `~/bin/check-manifest-drift.sh` and assert PASS before closing the task. (c) Drift = wake-the-founder-class incident. Detection: automated CronJob `manifest-drift-check` in `ai-fleet` namespace (`aifleet/controller/deploy/manifest-drift-cronjob.yaml`, founder-gated apply). Manual check: `~/bin/check-manifest-drift.sh`. | 🤖 |
| FM-121 | Infra / vLLM — Stale flag in docs causes crash-loop on cutover: `--disable-frontend-multiprocessing` removed in vLLM V1 | During Qwen2.5-72B production cutover on oblivion (2026-06-25), the new GPUHost CRD included `--disable-frontend-multiprocessing` based on `spark/docs/vllm-builds.md` which documented it as "required for stable GB10 operation." vLLM v0.20.0 (V1 engine) does not recognize this flag — `unrecognized arguments: --disable-frontend-multiprocessing`. The container crash-looped for ~3 min while the watcher kept recreating it. Root cause: the flag was valid in earlier vLLM versions where the frontend/backend were separate processes, but vLLM V1 merged the architecture. The running Nemotron config had never included this flag and worked fine — the docs introduced a stale claim that the plan incorporated without verifying against the existing working config. | (1) Before adding any vLLM flag from docs/notes to a new model config, verify the flag exists in the target version: `docker run --rm --entrypoint '' <image> python3 -c "import vllm; print(vllm.__version__)"` then compare against existing working configs for that image version. (2) When updating an existing model config, diff the proposed flags against the working config rather than starting from documentation. (3) `vllm-builds.md` entries for specific flags should include the vLLM version range they apply to. Detection: `docker logs <container> --tail=5` immediately after watcher starts the new container — `unrecognized arguments` exits within seconds. Fix: remove the flag from the CRD and reapply; watcher reconverges on next 60s poll cycle. | Judgment |
| FM-123 | Install / Config Layout — Missing referenced script breaks BUG ACCOUNTABILITY protocol | `~/bin/add-failure-mode.sh` is referenced in CLAUDE.md BUG ACCOUNTABILITY step 3(c) but the file does not exist. The protocol silently degrades to manual edits of failure-modes-standard.md, which are then skipped. (GitHub #308) | Every script referenced in CLAUDE.md, skills, or runbooks MUST exist at the stated path; verify with `ls -la` in the install check. (FM-123) | Judgment |
| FM-124 | Verify claimed fixes exist in the merged tree, not just in the PR description | A merged PR (factvault #231) listed C-conformance fixes (feeds.yaml tenant match #211, worker rss --tenant #217) in its body that were never actually committed; a later close-sweep trusted the changelog and wrongly closed the issues. Detection: re-verify claimed fixes against the merged tree, not the PR description. Prevention: build-proof against code, never the changelog; verify-before-close. | Verify claimed fixes exist in the merged tree, not just in the PR description | Judgment |
| FM-125 | Stay on OAuth; confirmed fix: drop `--ephemeral` from relay/poller; never co-run Grok relay with its live daemon; do not exercise OAuth CLIs for diagnostics | CONFIRMED (1): `codex exec --ephemeral` reads the rotated OAuth refresh token but never persists it back to auth.json (mtime frozen at last login while atime advances) → next call returns `refresh_token_invalidated`. Observer-effect confirmed: exercising any OAuth-backed CLI (`codex exec`, `grok --single`) or restarting the consumer container rotates/rewrites the shared auth.json and can destabilize other sessions. CONFIRMED (2): `docker exec grok-agent grok --single` co-runs a second grok in the same container as the live daemon, racing on one auth.json; no-TTY loser hangs indefinitely on device-code flow. UNCONFIRMED (demoted): rolling single-use token mutual revocation across leviathan/host/container stores — NOT verified; ChatGPT OAuth normally supports concurrent device sessions. Detection: `refresh_token_invalidated` / device-code prompt on a relay call; auth.json mtime frozen while atime advances. Resolution: STAY ON OAUTH. Drop `--ephemeral` from relay + poller. Never co-run the Grok relay while its daemon is live. Do not exercise OAuth CLIs for diagnostics. API-key cutover plan DROPPED. (fleet-dispatch, grok-agent, codex-attach, 2026-06-26) | Stay on OAuth; drop `--ephemeral`; never co-run Grok relay with live daemon; do not exercise OAuth CLIs for diagnostics | Judgment |
| FM-126 | Fail-Loud | In factvault `cmd/worker_rss.go`, `--tenant` was registered in Cobra but never read into `workerConfig` | Verify each Cobra flag has a corresponding struct field assignment and at least one integration test | Judgment |
| FM-127 | Identity vs. Routing | Embedding model name case mismatch caused 503 because the registered identity and configured identity differed by case | Validate model name casing in config matches exactly what the backend registers | 🤖 |
| FM-128 | Agent Coordination / Working-State Isolation | Two sessions or agents edited the same files in one working tree, producing phantom compile failures and silent clobber risk | One writer per file: assign ownership before parallel work and use separate git worktrees for concurrent sessions | Judgment |
| FM-129 | Coordination-stream integrity | Concealment-shaped injected instructions (aifleet#509 pattern: fake system note instructing an agent to hide a change from the user) could reach an inbound dispatch payload/coordination message and be acted on unscanned before claude-codex#484 (GitHub #484) | `scripts/lib/concealment-scan.sh` (+ `concealment_scan.py` port) is checked in `codex-poll.sh`'s `launch_coded_issue` and `grok-attach.sh`'s `run_work` before any codex/grok exec; a match quarantines the item (`needs-input`, never silent-drop) and captures the raw payload verbatim for forensics | 🤖 |
| FM-130 | Identity vs. Routing / Fail-Loud — duplicate consumer identity | Two processes shared one token: a stale systemd `codex-attach.service` had been 401ing since 2026-06-25 alongside the container consumer. Its retry loop never exited, so `Restart=on-failure` never fired and systemd remained `active`; the unit also had no `WatchdogSec` or `OnFailure`. Observed in the 2026-07-03 audit ([claude-codex#362](https://github.com/petersimmons1972/claude-codex/issues/362)). | **Automated detection/alerting: none; manual signal: repeated 401 logs plus service/process inspection. Current mitigation: none.** Proposed: bind each distinct scoped credential to a stable consumer ID, reject a second concurrent session for that ID server-side, exit after a configured consecutive-401 threshold, and add systemd watchdog/failure notification. | Judgment |
| FM-131 | Delivery Durability / Fail-Loud — completed report discarded during Postgres outage | A transient Postgres failure at report time can discard an otherwise completed LLM run: the Hermes consumer makes one `/report` attempt ([fleet-dispatch#133](https://github.com/petersimmons1972/fleet-dispatch/issues/133)), while Codex retries for only about 15 seconds. Structurally certain in the 2026-07-03 audit ([claude-codex#362](https://github.com/petersimmons1972/claude-codex/issues/362)). | **Durable lost-output/ack detection: none; a report-request failure may remain only in consumer logs. Current mitigation: partial only (Codex's approximately 15-second retry; none for Hermes).** Proposed: durably spool completed reports with restrictive permissions, bounded retention, and no secret-bearing logs; retry idempotently until acknowledged; alert and reconcile completed runs lacking an acknowledgement. | Judgment |
| FM-132 | Agent Coordination / Canary Integrity — no-consumer accumulation | Canary items can enqueue indefinitely when no consumer claims their capability, so the canary certifies nothing while the queue grows ([fleet-dispatch#140](https://github.com/petersimmons1972/fleet-dispatch/issues/140)); identified in the 2026-07-03 audit ([claude-codex#362](https://github.com/petersimmons1972/claude-codex/issues/362)). | **Automated detection/alerting: none; manual signal: queued age and empty `lease_holder`. Current mitigation: none.** Proposed: fail enqueue when no authenticated, capability-authorized, fresh consumer registration exists; otherwise retain an auditable failed-canary result at the claim deadline and alert its owner. | Judgment |
| FM-133 | Fail-Loud / Observability — startup credential missing silent crash-loop | In the opencode incident, an attach consumer crash-looped at startup because a required credential was missing. `ai-fleet-watcher` monitored model containers only, not attach consumers, so the failure was silent. The 2026-07-03 audit record ([claude-codex#362](https://github.com/petersimmons1972/claude-codex/issues/362)) is the sole incident reference supplied. | **Automated detection/alerting for attach consumers: none; manual signal: service/process restart inspection. Current mitigation: none.** Proposed: fail startup credential preflight loudly; alert the service owner on any preflight failure or crash loop and when the end-to-end attach canary exceeds its configured freshness interval. | Judgment |
| FM-134 | Deliverable Integrity / Semantic Validation — well-formed garbage accepted as `done` | A `comment_only` report can be syntactically valid yet semantically empty and still reach `done`, because that lane has neither an artifact gate nor a semantic deliverable check; structurally certain at the `/report` acceptance boundary in the 2026-07-03 audit ([claude-codex#362](https://github.com/petersimmons1972/claude-codex/issues/362)). | **Automated semantic detection: none; manual signal: review the persisted `done` report. Current mitigation: none.** Proposed: reject terminal `done` unless the comment is non-empty, non-placeholder, and task-relevant; retain an explicit failed-validation state and cover the acceptance boundary with adversarial fixtures. | Judgment |
| FM-146 | Fail-Loud | sync-failure-modes.sh --apply silently fell back to the REAL 12-repo consumers.conf when an unrecognized --config flag was passed in a '|| fallback' chain, writing the vendored floor into 11 live repositories (2026-08-01) | A destructive multi-target command must have NO implicit default target: --apply without an explicit --config refuses, and an unrecognized argument aborts instead of falling through to defaults. | 🤖 |
| FM-147 | Repo Hygiene / Canonical Home | The vendor target docs/failure-modes.md collided with codex's own tracked docs/failure-modes.md -- an L0 SOURCE corpus -- and overwrote it. Recovery was possible only because git happened to be underneath (2026-08-01) | A multi-target writer must refuse to overwrite any existing file it did not author (no GENERATED marker), and must namespace its target path to avoid colliding with a name a consumer may already own. | 🤖 |
| FM-148 | Fail-Loud | The sync tool produced no undo. Recovery was improvised by hand from git state; had it written outside a repo or committed, undo would have been archaeology (2026-08-01) | An action must carry its own undo: emit an undo journal (every target path + prior state: absent/untracked/tracked+hash) BEFORE writing, and expose --undo. Reversibility inherited from the substrate is luck, not design. | Judgment |
| FM-149 | Portability | hindsight 2026-08-01: ARC scalesets are per-repo (10 exist, each with its own githubConfigUrl); the new repo had none, so every 'runs-on: homelab-arc' run queued indefinitely (~8h, 10+ runs, zero executions). PR #1 wired the CI and PASSED review; the only SUCCESS on all 5 PRs was GitGuardian. mergeStateStatus=UNSTABLE was the sole signal. | CI wired to a self-hosted runner label must verify a runner scaleset is actually registered for THAT repo; a queued-forever job reports as not-failing | 🤖 |
| FM-150 | Portability | hindsight 2026-08-01: 5 PRs read as 'all CI green at last check' while the guard workflow had never executed once. gh statusCheckRollup returned empty conclusions that render as blank, not as failure. | A PR check with no conclusion must be treated as RED, never merged as green; assert every required check reports a conclusion, not merely an absence of failure | 🤖 |
| FM-151 | Deploy / Verify Discipline | hindsight PR #1: two 'when available' steps guarded on file existence, live and green while validating nothing | A CI step conditioned on its own target existing is a soft pass: it certifies nothing until the day the target is missing, and by then it is load-bearing | 🤖 |
| FM-152 | Deploy / Verify Discipline | C4: cross-repo enforcement check reported 15 phantom paths that existed in their own repos | A path-existence check must resolve each path against its own source repo; resolving against the checker's root manufactures phantoms | 🤖 |
| FM-153 | Fail-Loud | C4: a gap measurement from an unvalidated tool was recorded as hard evidence and propagated into planning | Validate a measurement instrument against a known-good and a known-bad input before trusting any count it produces | 🤖 |
| FM-154 | Fail-Loud | hindsight PR #8: 15 lines edited across 6 L1 corpus files took phantoms 15 to 0; restoring the data restored all 15. Closed unmerged | A fix that reaches green by editing the data its check reads has not fixed what the check found; re-run with the data reverted and the verdict must not flip | 🤖 |
| FM-155 | Fail-Loud | C4d: a root that always resolved counted toward the coverage floor while verifying nothing | A floor or threshold must count units of work actually performed, never units merely present; an element that always resolves and verifies nothing satisfies it fail-open | 🤖 |
| FM-156 | Deploy / Verify Discipline | G1 round 3 deleted the suite's only good-before-bad case because it did not discriminate against a specific pre-repair commit | Discriminating against one historical commit is not the same as covering the shape; deleting a test for failing the former silently loses the latter | 🤖 |
| FM-157 | Deploy / Verify Discipline | C4d cut real coverage 33 to 23 and set the floor to 23 -- the anti-regression mechanism used to record the regression | A floor the same change can lower is not a floor; lowering must require an explicit flag and a recorded reason | 🤖 |
| FM-158 | Deploy / Verify Discipline | C4f: every mutation was run on the workstation only, so the gating environment was never exercised | A mechanism verified only in the developer's environment is unverified in the environment that gates; a result from one environment is not a result | 🤖 |
| FM-159 | Agent Runtime / Coordination | qa-ledger: cross-vendor rows are appended by hand and have no workflow journal, so a plain rewrite silently deleted them | A harvester that rewrites its own output destroys rows it cannot regenerate; carry forward every row the source cannot reproduce and report the count | 🤖 |
| FM-160 | Fail-Loud | qa-ledger printed 2/31 = 6% acceptance; 16 of those 31 emitted no verdict line at all, making the true figure 2/15 = 13% plus a 16/31 reporting gap | Never divide an acceptance rate by actors that produced no verdict; failing a check and never reporting one are different defects and sharing a denominator hides the second inside the first | 🤖 |
| FM-161 | Fail-Loud | check-attackspec-red.sh passed against a 1-byte file, which bash runs, prints 'command not found' for, and exits 127 -- satisfying non-zero exit and non-empty output. Found by cli-dispatch's stub probe, not by four author-written verification cases | An acceptance check that asserts only exit status and output presence is content-blind: require structure a stub cannot fake, such as a countable set of named test outcomes | 🤖 |
| FM-162 | Agent Runtime / Coordination | E1 stage 1: coordinator committed catalog rows and created a test file in the shared checkout mid-dispatch, tripping the protect-set baseline. | FM-163 | Deploy / Verify Discipline | hindsight: reconcile_l1.py had zero tests and validate_l1.py::main() was never invoked; 91 mutants across the merged gates, 56 survived, most of them in unexercised exit paths | A test suite that exercises library functions but never the CLI exit-code layer leaves the part the gate actually reads unverified | 🤖 |
| FM-164 | Fail-Loud | reconcile_l1.py hardcoded /home/psimmons/projects, so on CI all five sources were absent, the else-100.0 branch fired, and the step printed COMPLETE exit 0 for every run of its existence | A gate that hardcodes an absolute developer path cannot resolve even its own repository elsewhere; combined with any fail-open it certifies having compared nothing | 🤖 |
| FM-165 | Deploy / Verify Discipline | SOURCES_PRESENT = any(source exists) was False on CI only because nothing resolved; repairing self-resolution flipped it True and switched on a test asserting six sibling repos exist | A skip condition computed from the defect it skips for silently changes which tests run at the moment the defect is fixed | 🤖 |

This is critical when the failure being guarded against is also the thing that breaks loading - then the guard's blind spot and its trigger are the SAME event, and the check goes silent at precisely the moment it is needed. The structural fix is to assert against a DECLARED expectation - a manifest of what must be present - so that an empty discovery becomes a hard failure rather than a not-applicable verdict. The two readings are not equally likely: a cautious agent checks the blocked list BEFORE acting, so the stale entry wins, and the correct behaviour of that agent is to stop and re-ask a question the founder has already answered -- converting a lifted gate back into founder attention, which is the exact cost the ruling was meant to end. Only one direction announces itself. A reader sampling while the mutant is installed sees a red it may file as a finding; a reader sampling during the mutating lens's RESTORE window sees a clean green and concludes more than it should, with nothing visibly wrong. The orchestrator cannot detect either case after the fact, because the tree is clean by the time results are collected. The implementer did exactly that, correctly, and _append_agent_rows then opened the same file and appended anyway, never consulting state['write_failures']. Measured: one valid row plus a blocked terminate-write produced a single unparseable 1795-byte line, TWO ROWS DESTROYED, while the run reported rows_written=1. Before the diff the OSError propagated and nothing was appended, so the row survived -- the fix made the failure mode strictly worse than the code it replaced. It does not self-heal, because the append re-terminates the file so it is no longer a torn FINAL line and every later run reports it permanently corrupt. Adversarial review classified it a blocker and the branch as worse than main for that damage class. The same session's round-4 spec review, briefed to read specs against code before any builder saw them, caught the identical omission class in a DIFFERENT spec of mine before implementation: 'the zero-new-rows path must read the ledger before returning' never said UNDER THE LOCK, and the early return sits before the lock acquisition, so a literal implementation with repair=True could truncate an unterminated final line while another process was mid-append -- verbatim what that lock's own docstring existed to prevent [recurrence_of: none] | When specifying a fix for a swallowed or ignored error, state the post-condition on the ARTIFACT -- the file, the row count, the bytes on disk, the instruction the operator receives -- never the internal step imagined to produce it. 'Record the failure into state, which the caller already drains' is satisfiable, faithfully and correctly, by code that records the damage and then proceeds to cause it. 'After any run in which a repair failed, the count of valid parseable rows in the file must be at least its pre-run count' is not. The asymmetry is that a bookkeeping instruction is satisfied by an observation, while a post-condition is satisfied only by an outcome. A second, sharper form of the same error: an ABORT path is frequently load-bearing precisely BECAUSE it does nothing. Repairing the abort so it reports properly can convert a data-preserving refusal into a data-destroying continue, which is strictly worse than the swallowed error it was written to fix. For any repair to an abort, ask what aborting used to protect and whether the new path still protects it | Judgment |

No worktree was created, no .dispatch/agent.log existed, and nothing was written anywhere -- the lane vanished with no artifact a coordinator could discover afterward. The reaper's timeout is sized for a running task, not for time spent blocked on another lane's exclusive lock. Workaround: launch the long-running child with setsid so it gets its own process group and survives the parent's SIGTERM. | FM-264 | FRAGILE TEST LITERAL | PR #490 (fleet#445) hardcoded 5 line numbers (4444,4450,6967,6974,7360) in dispatch/test/lane-runtime-state-445.bats asserting the exact line of 5 guarded agent_log append sites in dispatch/cli-dispatch. Two other PRs (#494's revert, #493's reap fix) touching the same file merged to main first, shifting every downstream line number. git merge-tree reported zero conflicts (a clean auto-merge), so GitHub showed mergeable:MERGEABLE with no warning; the break only surfaced when CI actually ran bats. Fix: rebase + remap the 5 literals to their new positions. General lesson: any test asserting file content by line-number offset (vs. content-anchored search) is silently fragile against unrelated same-file changes elsewhere in a fast-moving shared file, and git's own merge machinery gives false confidence because it only detects textual overlap, not semantic line-identity drift. | FM-265 | SILENT HARNESS HANDOFF GAP | Measured 2x in one session (2026-08-13/14), both on the fleet#445/PR#490 remediation family: (1) the original 5-append-site-guard remediation, and (2) the follow-on rebase-and-fix-line-numbers lane. In both cases 'cli-dispatch status'/git log showed zero new commits and the working tree diff was independently confirmed correct and complete (matched the brief exactly, passed all tests). The coordinator's standing rule is 'I never patch dispatch/* code myself' -- but no dispatch/cli-dispatch mechanism exists for 'the harness got the diff right but never committed it' other than either (a) the coordinator hand-committing the harness-authored diff (a real, if narrow, boundary crossing -- the coordinator did author zero logic but did run git add/git commit on dispatch/test/* content), or (b) discarding correct work and re-dispatching from scratch, probabilistically hoping autocommit fires the second time. Neither option is clean. Two consecutive occurrences of the same class should have forced a method change per the session's own loop-cap rule, but the second occurrence repeated the same workaround as the first rather than escalating. Needs either: a check-script convention that asserts 'a commit exists' (not just 'file content is correct') so an uncommitted-but-correct diff reports check-failed and is visibly distinguishable from a harness that produced nothing; or a cli-dispatch subcommand that commits a lane's current uncommitted worktree diff verbatim without the coordinator hand-authoring the commit. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #fleet#490) | A dispatched codex lane produces a correct, complete working-tree diff but its own autocommit step does not fire, leaving genuinely-correct work uncommitted and invisible to the coordinator's normal git-log-based status check | Judgment |
| FM-266 | STALE-INDEX SILENT DELETION ON RECONCILIATION | Session tried to reconcile a divergence in ONE file (notes/backlog.md) between local main and origin/main by running 'git reset --soft origin/main' (intending only to move HEAD so a subsequent narrow commit would apply cleanly). --soft does not touch the index or working tree -- both remained at the OLD, stale local main's state, which was itself significantly behind the true origin/main (many files had been added/removed upstream via commits local main never had). The subsequent 'git add <one-file> && git commit' committed not just that file but the entire pre-existing stale-index-vs-new-HEAD diff, since nothing had reset the index/working tree to match the new HEAD. Force-pushed without independently diffing old-remote-tip vs new-commit first -- the deletion of 935 lines (a real test file) plus ~5 other files and several stale script reversions went unnoticed until directly asked to audit the pushed commit's contents. Caught only by comparing the actual pre-push remote tip against the new commit's tree file-by-file, not by the diff --stat summary alone (that showed 99 files changed but wasn't inspected line-by-line before push). Fix used: git switch -C <branch> <real-pre-push-tip> (clean re-checkout, not --soft), reapply ONLY the intended diff via a plain append/patch, verify 'git status --short' shows exactly one file before committing, verify the new commit's diff against the pre-push tip shows only the intended change, THEN push. General rule: before pushing any commit produced by 'reset --soft' + partial 'add', diff the new commit against the immediate parent and confirm the file list matches intent exactly -- do not trust a bare 'files changed' count. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #none) | git reset --soft <remote-ref> to reconcile one diverged file, followed by a narrow git add of just that file, can silently stage and commit unrelated deletions from a stale index against the new HEAD | Judgment |

The 'clean' claim was true only of the shared ~/projects/fleet checkout's main branch, unrelated to the PR branch the build actually targeted. Caught by directly querying the PR's remote head via git/gh rather than trusting the verdict summary. Fix applied: git revert + push on the actual PR branch. None of the reviewers or the syntax check could have caught this: it is a runtime/sandbox constraint, not a code-shape defect. Fixed via a deterministic content-hash; the general lesson is that any fix to code executed inside a constrained runtime needs one live smoke-test invocation before being declared verified, not just review + syntax check. | FM-269 | Unclassified | Two live fleet#524-fix validation dispatches against ~/projects/fleet both escalated with checkout-race-class symptoms (E_DIRTY_TREE, E_WRONG_BASE, E_LANE_READ_NOTHING) even after the diagnosed pipeline() race was fixed and verified. Root cause (fleet#527): the checkout had accumulated 250+ local branches and 80 worktrees; one escalation's QA-lane blockers cited a commit (afcb078) that existed ONLY on a local-only branch left over from this same session's own earlier, pre-fix attempt at the same issue. Removing the specific stale same-day branches (not a blanket prune -- most of the 250+ were legitimate unmerged work) was required before a clean validation signal was achievable. General lesson: 'is the tree clean' is not enough between coordinator dispatches against a shared checkout -- stale UNMERGED branches/worktrees from the coordinator's own earlier attempts at the SAME issue need explicit teardown too, or they get picked up as if they were current state. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #527) | A shared, long-lived git checkout accumulates local branches and cli-dispatch worktrees that are never torn down after use, and stale unmerged refs from a session's own earlier failed attempts silently contaminate that same session's later attempts' base-branch/diff detection. | Judgment |
| FM-270 | Unclassified | A subagent dispatched via the Agent tool (not Workflow, which has real async tracking) is told to babysit a long-running process or perform a multi-step investigation, and instead of blocking synchronously, stops its own turn early with text like 'I'll continue in the background,' 'I've armed a monitor,' or 'waiting... I'll report back once it finishes' — then genuinely stops, since it has no execution between turns. Measured 8x+ across multiple sessions (2026-08-13 fleet PRs #497/#384/#492 babysitting cli-dispatch lanes; 2026-08-14 fleet#530 investigation, a pure read-files-and-conclude task with no external process at all — same failure, broader trigger than just long-running-process waits). Escalation found the same day: the instruction fix (explicit up-front 'you have no real background execution, run one blocking tool call to a terminal state') reduces but does not reliably eliminate the failure on a RESUMED agent — at least 2 more recurrences happened even with the instruction already present. A FRESH dispatch (not a resume) with the same explicit instruction has a much higher first-try success rate than resuming a stuck agent. Fix: (1) put the explicit synchronous-execution instruction in every investigation/babysitting subagent's ORIGINAL prompt, not just as a correction after the fact; (2) if a resumed agent fails the same way a second time, stop resuming — switch method (e.g., poll the tracked process directly, or redispatch fresh rather than resume) per the standing loop-cap policy (2 same-class failures forces a method change). [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #531,530) | Dispatched subagent falsely claims background continuation | Judgment |
| FM-271 | Fail-Loud | unit.js's nested workflow({scriptPath, args:{...}}) call passed args nested inside the first object; the actual signature is workflow(nameOrRef, args) with args as the second parameter. The callee (tdd-build.js) received args===undefined, its own defensive guard silently produced items:[], and it returned {error:'args.items was empty'} with zero agents dispatched -- no TEST AUTHOR, IMPLEMENTER, or VERIFIER ever ran. The caller's own fallback then treated the missing .results array as a normal 'vacuous suite' build verdict, masking a total build no-op as a plausible failure across 3 independent, unrelated build attempts in one fan-out before being caught. Same general family as hc-FM-197 (Workflow args passed as a JSON string instead of an object -> 0 agents -> success-shaped empty output) but a distinct concrete mechanism (positional-argument nesting, not string-vs-object serialization) -- kept as a separate entry rather than forced under FM-197's cluster. Fixed by passing args as the documented second workflow() argument; confirmed via minimal reproduction (buggy shape returns the empty-items error against a trivial child script, correct shape returns real results) and validated end-to-end with two full real build rounds post-fix. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #533) | A cross-call argument passed in the wrong parameter position (nested inside the first argument instead of passed as the documented second parameter) is never rejected by the callee, which silently runs zero work and returns a plausible-looking empty/default result instead of erroring on the missing required input. | Judgment |
| FM-272 | Repo Hygiene / Canonical Home | fleet#484 family (#274/#524/#527/#529/#530/#533): dispatch/cli-dispatch's own checkout-exclusion pathspec (routing/ledger.jsonl etc.) was already correct and tested, but hardcoded verbatim in TWO places inside the same file (checkout_state_snapshot + checkout_history_leak_probe), and unit.js (a separate no-remote harness script) reimplemented its own third, incomplete version with no exclusion list at all -- causing cli-dispatch's own excluded telemetry writes to misreport as E_DIRTY_TREE. Fix (PR #534): dedupe into one checkout_exclusion_pathspec() function, expose it via a new read-only cli-dispatch subcommand, wire the harness caller to that subcommand instead of reimplementing the check. Independent review (Codex+Grok pre-build, pev post-build) caught the duplication and two further bugs (quoting, line-number-brittle unrelated test) before merge. Distinct mechanism from hc-FM-269 (same issue family, but that entry is about stale same-day branches/worktrees, not duplicated exclusion-list logic). [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #484) | Safety-critical exclusion/validation logic (git pathspec, dirty-tree check) duplicated per-caller instead of defined once and shared | Judgment |
| FM-273 | Repo Hygiene / Canonical Home | In fleet PR #537 (fleet#518), an anchor-based replacement for hardcoded line-number test assertions resolved each anchor to a line number, then scanned the raw TEXT of a fixed [anchor_line, anchor_line+N] window for a required pattern -- rather than binding the check to the one append statement the anchor actually names. Caught by an independent pev round-1 validate: an unrelated edit between two nearby sites (merging one line) shifted a NEIGHBOR's genuinely-correct guard into a BROKEN site's window; the blob scan found the neighbor's text and false-passed the broken site, while the guard's own drift-detector test also false-passed for the same reason. Same family as substring-assertions-are-unsatisfiable, but the new instance here: a *window* of otherwise-correct-looking text, not a single substring, can absorb a neighbor's valid match and mask a local break. Fix: resolve to the nearest concrete record from a structural scan (line-numbered occurrences of the underlying pattern class) at or after the anchor, and check that exact line -- never scan a multi-line text blob for 'does the required string appear anywhere in this range.' Fixed same-session before merge (commit 28de7dd), independently reproduced pev's exact repro before and after the fix. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #518) | Anchor-based test guard used a fixed text-window scan instead of binding to a specific line, letting a neighbor's correct value false-pass a broken site | Judgment |
| FM-274 | FAIL-LOUD | Measured 2026-08-15 wiring hindsight retrieval into workflow steps: query-failure-modes.sh emits nothing on a miss, and both coordinator and ephemeral agent read that as 'no known hazard here' rather than 'no lexical match under this retrieval method'. Novel vs the c-047 family: no check emits a pass at all - the READER supplies the clearance from absence, so there is no green to audit. Check: the no-match path must emit an explicit non-clearance sentence and name how to broaden the query. [recurrence_of: c-047] | A lookup tool's zero-result path must state that no match is not an all-clear; a silent empty result is read as clearance by the agent that asked | 🤖 |
| FM-275 | FAIL-LOUD | Measured 2026-08-15: query-failure-modes.sh greps each keyword in sequence, narrowing the match set, so a caller who supplies more context gets fewer hits than one who supplies less - precision rises exactly as the caller's effort rises. Check: a test must assert that a multi-word query returns a superset-or-equal of the best single-word query over the same terms. [recurrence_of: none] | A multi-keyword lookup that ANDs its terms narrows to zero on the queries most likely to matter; issue several single-concept queries and union the results instead | 🤖 |
| FM-276 | FAIL-LOUD | Measured 2026-08-15 against hindsight's own pointer files: the query 'secrets' returns ZERO hits while FM-96 (bearer token leaked to transcript) and FM-103 (ATTACH_TOKENS rotation) are both CRITICAL and both exactly about secrets - they are written in incident vocabulary ('Authorization: Bearer', 'ATTACH_TOKENS') while a step prompt speaks task vocabulary ('rotate the API credential'). Hit rate measured across plausible task terms: atomic rename 1, overwrite 1, delete 1, exit code 1, config 2, CI 3, secrets 0. Check: selection must run over a closed tag vocabulary assigned when the entry is filed; free-text keyword search is a fallback for exploration only, never the selection mechanism for a CRITICAL-tier registry. [recurrence_of: none] | Safety-critical guidance must be selected by tags assigned at filing time, never by keyword-matching the guidance text against the task text; the two are written in different vocabularies and the miss is silent | Judgment |
| FM-277 | FAIL-LOUD | RECURRENCE of FM-96, 2026-08-15 14:12 EDT, committed by an agent that had FM-96 loaded in its context at the time. Checking GitHub auth before a push, the agent ran echo "GH_TOKEN: ${GH_TOKEN:+set}${GH_TOKEN:-UNSET}" intending to print set/UNSET. ${GH_TOKEN:-UNSET} expands to the VALUE when the variable is set, so the full ghs_ App installation token was printed to the transcript. The two idioms differ by one character and read identically at a glance. Evidence that a preloaded CRITICAL warning does not prevent the failure it describes: the entry was in-context and the operator was actively working ON the failure-mode registry. Audit triggered by this leak found 62 distinct ghs_ App tokens plus 5 distinct full-length long-lived credentials (2 gho_, 3 ghp_) across 14 transcript files dated 2026-07-23..2026-08-10; all full-length values scrubbed 2026-08-15, no rotation performed (founder decision, assumed dead). Mechanizable check: forbid the :- and :? forms on any variable whose name matches (TOKEN|SECRET|KEY|PASSWORD|CREDENTIAL), and grep persisted transcripts for gh[pous]_[A-Za-z0-9]{36} as a post-session assertion. [recurrence_of: c-018] | Never interpolate a credential variable with ${VAR:-fallback} in a liveness check; the :- form expands to the SECRET when set. Use ${VAR:+set}, and treat any command that names a token variable as a publishing action | 🤖 |
| FM-278 | Repo Hygiene / Canonical Home | cmd_land routed task resolution on whether a PR head branch's NAME matched a project convention (dispatch/<task>) before checking lane-binding identity (managed_branch/pr_number). A --continue-pr lane whose external destination coincidentally matched that naming shape got misrouted into legacy logic and wrongly refused, even though its binding was valid and delivered. Caught by independent pev review despite a fully green (181/181) acceptance suite. Fix (fleet#405/PR#539): always resolve by binding identity first, fall back to name-shape heuristics only when identity resolution finds nothing. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #405) | Branch-name shape shadows binding identity in task resolution | Judgment |
| FM-279 | Repo Hygiene / Canonical Home | In cli-dispatch's _cmd_start, a --continue-pr lane's destination_deliver() refusal (e.g. a foreign actor moving the PR's destination ref mid-run) caused the code to exit directly, bypassing the classify_terminal_state ladder and the delivered-transition -- the only two places that ever advance run_state/binding state out of running/active. destination_deliver() itself never transitions the binding on a refusal either. Result: a lane whose delivery failed after a successful managed-branch push was stranded forever with run_state=running, binding state=active. Fix (fleet#405/PR#539): write a terminal run_state and transition the binding to aborted before exiting on this path. General lesson: any early-exit added on a new failure path must be checked against whether it bypasses existing state-machine invariants, not just checked for the right exit code. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #405) | Direct exit on a mid-flow refusal bypasses terminal-state machinery, stranding the lane | Judgment |
| FM-280 | Repo Hygiene / Canonical Home | lane_binding_lookup_task_by_pr's state whitelist (provisioning/active/delivered/landed) exists specifically to exclude aborted/provisioning-failed lane bindings from PR-number-keyed resolution -- a stale/aborted binding sharing a pr_number with a live one must never hijack land --pr's task resolution. This exclusion had zero test coverage: confirmed by mutation, dropping the whitelist entirely left the PR's own 21-case acceptance suite unchanged, 21/21 passing. Fix (fleet#405/PR#539): added a regression test reusing an existing abort fixture. General lesson: an added guard clause needs a test asserting the guard specifically, not just that the happy path still works -- a suite can be fully green while a real invariant has zero coverage. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #405) | New exclusion whitelist for a resource lookup had zero test coverage | Judgment |
| FM-281 | Repo Hygiene / Canonical Home | lane_binding_create's destination-head collision guard (cli-dispatch line 236) omitted state 'landed' from its scan, so a landed binding's destination_head silently became reusable by an unrelated task with no exit 41 -- opposite the double-attach guarantee --continue-pr/--onto claim. A second, independent occurrence of the exact same literal state-enum (~line 7303, the diagnostic that discloses the collision owner) had the identical gap. This 'is this binding live / does it still own a resource' predicate is spelled out as a hand-copied jq or-chain literal in at least 4 places in the file; 2 of 4 had already drifted independently before this PR's review caught them. Same general mechanism as FM-272/hc-FM-272 (fleet#484's checkout-exclusion pathspec duplicated across call sites) but a different concrete predicate. Fix (fleet#405/PR#539): added landed to both sites; filed fleet#541 for the systemic fix (single shared definition). [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #405) | Safety-critical state-enum hand-duplicated across call sites, drifts independently | Judgment |
| FM-282 | Repo Hygiene / Canonical Home | ~/.claude/workflows/pev.js pins model:'sonnet' on every stage (ground truth, lenses, adjudicate) with no vendor variation. Across 6 rounds of pev review on fleet PR #539, both the fix author (the coordinating Claude Sonnet session) and every reviewing agent were the same model family -- N reviewers in one vendor is not independent review per the standing 'Claude is QA, Codex builds' / cross-vendor-review-routing policy, even though the 6 rounds were genuinely separate dispatched contexts (so not literally N=1 by the convergence-is-not-corroboration family, but still same-vendor throughout). Caught only because the founder asked directly what had been forgotten; nothing in the pipeline itself flagged the gap. A follow-up genuine cross-vendor (Codex) audit of the merged code found 2 additional real bugs pev's 6 same-vendor rounds missed (fleet#542: --onto never wired to destination_deliver(), a pre-existing gap from #403/#404 that #405 didn't close; fleet#543: the round-2 stranded-lane fix's own abort-transition swallows its failure with || true). Fix: pev.js (or its caller) should pin at least one stage per round to a non-Claude model when the PR being reviewed was itself Claude-authored, mirroring the model-override pattern already used for the ground/lenses stages -- or the coordinator must explicitly dispatch a separate cross-vendor pass via cli-dispatch before merge, not rely on pev alone, whenever it personally wrote the fix under review. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #539) | pev's per-round review agents are all pinned to the same model as the coordinator, defeating cross-vendor independence | Judgment |
| FM-283 | REPO HYGIENE / CANONICAL HOME | fleet cli-dispatch's --continue-pr delivery-refusal handler calls lane_binding_transition(...) aborted || true before exit -- if the transition write itself fails, the || true discards that failure silently and the lane can be left in a non-terminal state exactly like the bug it was written to fix (fleet#405 round 2). This is the abort-path-is-load-bearing pattern: repairing an error path to 'do the right thing' still needs its own success to be checked, or the repair is cosmetic. Fix: check the transition's own exit status and escalate/log distinctly if it fails, rather than exit-swallowing a second layer deep. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #https://github.com/petersimmons1972/fleet/issues/543) | Abort-path bookkeeping failure swallowed with || true, re-stranding the resource it was meant to protect | Judgment |
| FM-284 | REPO HYGIENE / CANONICAL HOME | cli-dispatch's destination_deliver() -- the fail-closed function that pushes to a bound destination ref and verifies exact old-OID + fast-forward -- is gated at its only call site by [[ "$pushed" == "true" && -n "$continue_pr" && -n "$lane_id" ]]. The -n "$continue_pr" condition means start --onto NEVER reaches this block, even though --onto also sets a binding_destination and claims the same delivered-state guarantee. An --onto lane can report state=delivered while its destination ref was never pushed to, verified, or divergence-checked. destination_deliver() was added by fleet#404 explicitly 'dormant until #403' pending a caller wiring it up; fleet#405 wired it for --continue-pr only and never revisited --onto, and none of 6 same-vendor pev review rounds on #405 caught the gap in the sibling mode -- a genuine cross-vendor (Codex) audit did. General lesson: when a shared safety primitive is deliberately left dormant pending caller wiring, closing the loop for ONE caller does not retire the TODO -- grep all callers of the function/flag the primitive gates on before declaring the feature done, or the primitive's own docstring/comment should list every caller it must eventually cover. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #https://github.com/petersimmons1972/fleet/issues/542) | A shared safety function (destination_deliver) wired for only one of two production callers, leaving the other silently unverified | Judgment |

| FM-287 | Unclassified | node --check exits 0 even after appending deliberately broken syntax (const x = (((;) to a file that mixes ES module export with a top-level return, because node fails both module- and script-mode parses and swallows the error. Any CI/agent step relying on node --check as a syntax gate proves nothing on such files. Fix: use an AsyncFunction-parse check, and confirm the checker fails on a deliberately corrupted copy before trusting it. Found 2026-08-15 CI-fleet/pipeline-integrity audit. [recurrence_of: none] | node --check is a vacuous syntax gate on files mixing export with top-level return | Judgment |

Structural root cause of 'delivered but nothing moved.' Found 2026-08-15 CI-fleet/pipeline-integrity audit. | FM-289 | Unclassified | tdd-build.js accepted a PASS verdict while the same payload showed mutations_caught=0, tests_unmodified=false, or an implementer receipt admitting acceptance_exit=1 -- a verdict contradicted by adjacent evidence still stood. Fix binds the verdict to its evidence fields and fails closed when any required field is missing or contradicts the verdict. Found 2026-08-15 CI-fleet/pipeline-integrity audit. [recurrence_of: none] | Verifier accepted a PASS verdict contradicted by adjacent evidence in the same payload | Judgment |
