Counters had reached 70 and 224 restarts before diagnosis. Found 2026-08-15 CI-fleet/pipeline-integrity audit. [recurrence_of: none] | Design-correct restart behavior misread by a supervisor as failure, throttling healthy throughput | Judgment |
| FM-291 | Unclassified | GitHub sets run_started_at ~= created_at, so RUN-level CI queue delay is identically zero for every repo -- measured at run level, 'no warm pools needed' is exactly backwards. At JOB level, one repo showed a 27-minute median queue wait, 25x worse than any other, invisible at run granularity. Always confirm the metric can express the condition being investigated. Found 2026-08-15 CI-fleet/pipeline-integrity audit. [recurrence_of: none] | Measuring CI queue delay at run level instead of job level inverts the conclusion | Judgment |
| FM-292 | Unclassified | Long-lived non-interactive processes capture the GitHub App credential in their environment at spawn and hold it past its 1-hour TTL, while a freshly refreshed one sits in the on-disk cache beside them. The refresh hook rides on PROMPT_COMMAND, which is interactive-only, so background jobs, cron, and dispatched subagents never pick it up. Compounded twice: cron has no BASH_ENV so it inherits nothing at all, and cli-dispatch:5931 ADVISES falling back to a different identity on 401, encoding the violation in tooling. Measured 2026-08-15: the personal OAuth quota was driven to 0/5000 and re-drained to 0 within minutes of reset while the App pool sat at 7395/7500 unused. Downstream blast radius: starved lens agents returned null, which a vacuous merge gate (FM-285) then treated as unanimous PASS, so one dead credential produced unsound merges. Detection: check the rate-limit LIMIT, not the remaining (7500=App, 5000=OAuth); a 5000 in a session that believes it is on the App is proof it is not. Fix must be mechanical, not procedural: this is the 6th occurrence (2026-07-29, 07-30 x3, 08-10, 08-15) with a 100 percent miss rate for background sessions, and every prior occurrence closed by restating the rule. Fix the acquisition path and delete the advised fallback; per-harness refresh timers must exist for ALL harnesses (grok had none until 2026-08-15). [recurrence_of: none] | Harness credential inherited at spawn, never re-synced (6th recurrence; recurrence of hc-FM-232) | Judgment |
| FM-293 | Agent Runtime / Coordination | Fleet #530: a 3-second Codex-shaped fixture never invoked cli-dispatch or Grok, so its late artifact proved only its own injected timeout; the real lane must capture actual CLI/Grok PID, PGID, watchdog, journal, and terminal-record evidence. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #530) | A synthetic child wrapper cannot certify the lifecycle of the real dispatched harness | Judgment |
| FM-294 | AGENT RUNTIME / COORDINATION | Measured 2026-08-15/16 (session 817d0e1f, CI-fleet audit): ~/.bash_env minted the Claude GitHub App installation token unconditionally on every non-interactive bash startup, so Codex-harness and Grok-harness processes inherited GH_TOKEN and authenticated to GitHub AS psimmons-claude-worker[bot]. Every commit, comment and API call they made was attributed to another harness, silently destroying the per-harness attribution that independent-review routing depends on (an agent could not tell it was borrowing an identity because gh auth status showed a valid App token). Detection: compare the acting bot login in a GitHub event against the harness that produced it; a single harness login across all harnesses is proof. Fix applied: route the mint on harness detection with default-deny (no harness detected = no token) rather than a single hardcoded harness. Related family: FM-292 (background/cron sessions falling through to founder OAuth) - same root surface (~/.bash_env + PROMPT_COMMAND refresh), different failure direction: FM-292 loses the App identity downward to OAuth, this one imposes ONE harness identity sideways onto all others. [recurrence_of: none] | A shared credential-mint path must select the credential by DETECTED HARNESS with default-deny, never mint one harness credential unconditionally for every shell | Judgment |
| FM-295 | AGENT RUNTIME / COORDINATION | Measured 2026-08-15/16 (session 817d0e1f). cli-dispatch line 5931 advises operators and agents to retry with `env -u GH_TOKEN gh ...` when an App-token call returns 401. That silently swaps the GitHub App identity for the founder personal OAuth identity: the call then succeeds, so nothing looks wrong, while attribution moves to a human and the personal 5000/hr quota absorbs fleet traffic. Filed as fleet#546. The failure is not that an agent broke a rule - the rule (App tokens exclusively) was correctly recorded and repeatedly restated - it is that the TOOL taught the violation at exactly the moment of failure, when an agent is most likely to follow instructions verbatim. General check: grep tooling error paths and docs for remediation advice that removes a credential, lowers a permission, disables a check, or skips a gate; that advice is a defect, not documentation. Related: FM-292 records the same advice as one compounding cause of the 6th recurrence of OAuth quota starvation; this entry isolates the pattern so it is findable outside the GitHub-auth context. [recurrence_of: none] | A tool must never document a fallback that downgrades identity or authority as the remedy for its own auth error; remediation text inside tooling is an instruction, and it will be followed | Judgment |
| FM-296 | AGENT RUNTIME / COORDINATION | Measured 2026-08-15/16 (session 817d0e1f, CI-fleet audit). A coordinator brief listed a KNOWN FACTS block; three of its entries were false and the executing agents spent budget falsifying them instead of doing the assigned work: (1) "armies CI has been failing for 4 months" - it had not; (2) "fleet-sync targets 404 because the repos were deleted" - they had been TRANSFERRED, not deleted; (3) "the telemetry units are instinct-project detritus" - zero overlap with instinct. One of the wrong root causes was published to fleet#549 before correction, so the error escaped the session into a public issue. Cost shape: a wrong fact in a brief is worse than an absent one, because the agent trusts it and reasons FORWARD from it, and the correction arrives only if the agent happens to trip over ground truth. Check: in any brief, each factual claim carries a command or artifact reference the executor can re-run in one step, or it is written as "unverified - confirm first". [recurrence_of: none] | A dispatch brief may not present unverified premises as KNOWN FACTS; every asserted fact in a brief is either carried with its verifying artifact or labelled a hypothesis to test | Judgment |
| FM-297 | AGENT RUNTIME / COORDINATION | Measured 2026-08-15/16 (session 817d0e1f). ~/HANDOFF.md - a file this session read at startup - already carried an entry from the fleet#405 work recording that Claude had personally authored every fix instead of dispatching Codex, violating the standing "Claude is QA, not builder" ruling. In this session I again personally authored the merge-gate fixes in ~/.claude/workflows (commits d407cfa, 4b05bf6, ffcd8ae, 0a4270f) rather than dispatching a builder. The prior occurrence was in context, in a file loaded before any work began, describing exactly the act about to be repeated. This is the same shape as FM-277 (an agent leaked a token while holding FM-96 in context) and cluster c-018: preloaded warnings have a measured miss rate near 100% at the moment of action, because the agent is attending to the task, not the registry. Check: for any rule of the form "role X must not do Y", place a mechanical gate at the point of action (a dispatch precondition, a pre-commit author check, a workflow step that refuses) - counting on the rule being loaded is not a control. [recurrence_of: c-018] | A failure mode recorded in an always-loaded file does not prevent its own recurrence; the standing rule it encodes must be enforced by a runtime gate or a dispatch-time check, not by being read | Judgment |
| FM-298 | AGENT RUNTIME / COORDINATION | Measured 2026-08-15/16 (session 817d0e1f). Four merge-gate defects in ~/.claude/workflows were each proven by a discriminating test run by hand: pev.js returned MERGE on 0/3 lenses before d407cfa and BLOCKED after; pr-whole.js returned MERGE-READY on 0/3 lenses before 4b05bf6; tdd-build.js accepted a self-reported PASS before ffcd8ae; unit.js/lib/converge.js accepted non-executed acceptance evidence before 0a4270f. Every one of those tests exists only as a transcript command. ~/.claude/workflows has no CI, no pre-commit hook, and no test runner, so the next edit to pev.js can silently reinstate the vacuous-quantifier bug and nothing anywhere will notice - the proof does not survive the session that produced it. This is the concrete form of "enforce in runtime, not policy": the code was fixed and the ENFORCEMENT was not, and only the enforcement is what makes the fix durable. Check: for every fix, name the automatic path that will re-run its discriminating test; if the answer is "I ran it", the item is not done. [recurrence_of: none] | A defect proven fixed by a hand-run discriminating test is not closed until that test runs in an automatic path; an unwired proof expires the moment the session ends | Judgment |
| FM-299 | AGENT RUNTIME / COORDINATION | Measured 2026-08-15/16 (session 817d0e1f), two independent instances in one session, both caught only because the executing agent overruled the brief. (1) I instructed an agent to verify workflow-script integrity with `node --check`; on files that mix ES-module export with a top-level return, node --check exits 0 even with `const x = (((;` appended - the prescribed method cannot fail, so it would have certified corrupted files (FM-287). (2) I specified measuring CI queue delay at RUN level; GitHub sets run_started_at ~= created_at, making run-level queue delay identically zero by construction, which would have inverted the ARC runner-sizing conclusion to "no warm pools needed" (FM-291). In both cases the QUESTION was sound and the METHOD was vacuous, and the vacuity was invisible to the author precisely because he chose the method for its plausibility. Checks: (a) briefs state the question plus what a PASS and a FAIL must each look like, leaving method to the executor; (b) any prescribed method ships with a positive control - show the method failing on a known-bad input before trusting it on the real one; (c) executors are told explicitly that overruling a method whose blind spot they can demonstrate is expected behaviour, not scope creep. [recurrence_of: none] | A brief must specify the QUESTION and its acceptance criterion, not the verification METHOD; a coordinator-chosen method carries a blind spot the coordinator cannot see, and the executor must be authorised to overrule it | Judgment |
| FM-300 | Unclassified | fleet PR #558: cmd_acceptance stripped */cli-dispatch-bg-guard.* from PATH so run_one_check would resolve the real setsid, but BACKGROUND_GUARD_DIR was unset in the fresh process, so item commands also got real setsid/nohup. PoC: --item "esc=setsid bash -c 'sleep 19; echo leaked' &" returned {"exit_code":0} while the work kept running in a new session/pgid; bg_reap_process_group only reaps the check's own pgid. A false zero in the very mechanism that decides whether anything is green. In-group nohup was correctly refused with 45, which is what made the escape look safe. Found by cross-vendor review (Grok) after CI green and coordinator-executed acceptance both passed. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #547) | An acceptance runner that strips a background-guard shim from PATH must re-arm the guard for the ITEM command, or genuinely backgrounded work escapes refusal and reports exit 0 | Judgment |
| FM-301 | Unclassified | fleet #560: a --harness grok review lane posted its VERDICT comment as psimmons-claude-worker. The review was genuinely Grok's (12.6KB of Grok analysis, findings correct) but the identity was Claude's. The merge gate requires a named reviewer of a different vendor than the builder, and the only durable externally-checkable part of that evidence is the comment author -- so a Grok review of a Claude-built PR reads to any later auditor as Claude reviewing Claude (FM-282). Worse than an obviously same-vendor review because it looks compliant. Recurrence of cross-harness token borrowing through a different path (grok lane rather than codex). [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #560) | A review lane's GitHub identity must match its harness, and it must fail closed rather than borrow an ambient token, or cross-vendor independence becomes unverifiable | Judgment |
| FM-302 | Unclassified | fleet #555: a grok lane reported status=agent-failed, commits=0, exit 13 -- while the work was complete and correct (3 bats files, 762 lines, gate passing 41/41). Its sandbox seccomp denied writes to .git/objects, refs and index.lock, so it finished and had no way to deliver. agent-failed with commits=0 reads as a capability failure, so the natural response is to re-dispatch, which burns the work again and fails identically because the cause is environmental and deterministic. A real outcome recorded as an absence. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #555) | A lane that completes its work but cannot commit must report a status distinct from 'the agent failed', naming the recoverable artifact | Judgment |
| FM-303 | Unclassified | fleet #559: --check-script runs inside the lane worktree, but dispatch/test/setup_suite.bash refuses by design to run the bats suite from a lane worktree (fleet#251/#207). So the most natural acceptance command in this repo -- ./dispatch/test/run-suite.sh <file> -- is structurally unpassable as a lane check. Lane issue-547 reported check-failed on an implementation that was correct on every required point and 10/10 green from a non-lane cwd. The coordinator's next step for check-failed is re-dispatch or switch-vendor; both would have discarded correct work. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #559) | An acceptance gate must be runnable in the environment where it will be enforced; a check that cannot pass in-lane fails correct work as if it were wrong | Judgment |
| FM-304 | Unclassified | fleet PR #554: a 41-case characterization suite for parse_toml/cmd_doctor/cmd_lint passed 41/41 with CI green and coordinator-executed acceptance green. Its parse_toml section was titled 'blank lines and # comments' but supplied only a standalone comment line and a value with no same-line comment. Mutating parse_toml to strip a trailing # before matching -- the exact mutation the dispatch brief had NAMED as required coverage -- left all 17 cases passing. Caught only by cross-vendor review. Three of four mutations were caught, which is what made the suite look adequate. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #512) | A characterization test must be mutation-verified against the specific refactor slip it claims to lock, or it passes green while failing to notice that exact change | Judgment |
| FM-305 | Unclassified | fleet PR #557: a detector banning mis-cited calibration figures used GROUP_RE = digits separated by bare slashes. The repo's own gate comments write '11 / 17 / 23' with spaces, so re-inserting the verbatim pre-fix sentences in house style passed clean -- a one-character style change restored the bug under a green suite. Its equivalence rule banned only 'exact'/'identical', so 'the same numbers as' and 'is equivalent to' laundered the identical claim. A synonym blacklist keeps losing this race; inverting the rule (any non-registered figure in a linkvault sentence is a violation unless it is a pure self-description) is the durable shape. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #516) | A text guard must be tested against the codebase's own house style, not only the exact wording of the bug it was written for | Judgment |
| FM-306 | Unclassified | 2026-08-16 fleet campaign: fix round 2 for fleet#516 was dispatched with --base origin/main instead of the PR branch. The lane lost round 1's yaml and doc corrections, and round 2's brief explicitly forbade it from redoing them, so its check failed in 2 seconds against unfixed inputs with 0 commits. Round 1 had got away with the same mistake only because its agent happened to redo the doc fixes from scratch. Coordinator error, not an agent failure. [recurrence_of: none] | A fix round must be based on the PR branch it is fixing, never on the base branch, or it silently discards the earlier round's work | Judgment |
| FM-307 | Unclassified | fleet #556: cli-dispatch snapshots the shared parent repo before and after a lane and sets leak=true on any mismatch. Ordinary coordinator commands in that repo while a lane was live (git fetch, gh, rg, cli-dispatch status) tripped it twice in one session, each time failing an otherwise-successful lane. The report was self-contradictory on its face -- leak=true with checkout_leak_detected=false and leak_commits=[] -- and named nothing that actually differed, so it cost real time to interpret and taught the reader to ignore it. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #556) | A leak or tamper guard that fires on the coordinator's own routine activity trains its reader to discount it, which is how a real leak later gets waved through | Judgment |
| FM-308 | Unclassified | 2026-08-16 fleet campaign: four review lanes were dispatched with --base origin/main. Three improvised different routes to the PR head (isolated archive, git archive/show, temp clone at /tmp) and one refused outright because git rev-parse HEAD returned the base. The refusal was correct behaviour but produced VERDICT: fail from a trusted identity, which then blocked cli-dispatch land on a sound PR -- a coordinator dispatch error wearing the costume of a review failure. The improvisations are the quieter hazard: a reviewer that improvised wrongly would produce a confident review of the wrong code and nothing in the lane result would contradict it. [recurrence_of: none] | A review lane must be placed at the commit under review; if it is handed the base instead, reviewers either improvise a fetch or fail closed, and the refusal is indistinguishable from a defect | Judgment |
| FM-309 | Unclassified | fleet PR #558 closed the setsid process-group escape by arming the background guard for the item command, verified: --item "esc=setsid ... &" went from exit_code 0 with a live orphan to 45 with none. But the guard works by shimming PATH lookups, so /usr/bin/setsid still escapes and still reports exit_code 0. Correctly judged a pre-existing limit of run_one_check rather than a regression, so the PR merged -- but it leaves a live false zero in the mechanism that now decides whether anything is green. Durable fix is a post-execution check that no descendant session survived, not an enumeration of detacher names, which loses the same race a synonym blacklist does. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #547) | A guard implemented by shimming PATH catches only the idiomatic invocation; an absolute-path call bypasses it entirely | Judgment |
| FM-310 | Unclassified | A dispatch acceptance gate resolved the tree under test as REPO=${1:-/home/psimmons/projects/fleet}. cli-dispatch stages the check script OUTSIDE the worktree and invokes it with NO argument, so the default won every in-lane run and the gate inspected the coordinator's UNFIXED main checkout rather than the agent's worktree. The gate reported check-failed for a CORRECT implementation twice in a row (fleet#540 attempt 1 and its retry); those two failures looked like a same-class IMPLEMENTATION failure and would have triggered a vendor switch under the loop cap, spending a second lane to re-fail against the same broken gate. Verified directly: with the worktree passed as an argument the script exits 0; with no argument, from inside that worktree, it exits 1 at the first probe. The implementation satisfied the positive control, all 8 direct behaviour probes, and 21 bats cases. SIGNATURE: a check runnable BOTH by a coordinator and inside a lane must resolve its target from its own cwd, never a default path, and must refuse loudly with a distinct non-zero exit when it cannot locate the tree - a gate that cannot find its own tree must never be able to report a pass. The direction here was fail-CLOSED, the only reason it was survivable; the identical defect in a gate whose default tree already satisfies the property fails OPEN and silently passes every broken implementation. DIAGNOSTIC LESSON: two consecutive same-class lane failures must be classified impl-caused vs gate-caused BEFORE the loop cap's method change is applied, because the cap's prescribed remedy (switch vendor) is exactly wrong for a gate-caused failure. Second occurrence of this shape in one session - the same hardcoded-path defect was caught pre-dispatch in the fleet#516 adversarial probe, where it would have made a lane test the coordinator's tree instead of its own. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #540) | Acceptance gate defaults to a hardcoded checkout, so every in-lane run tests the coordinator's tree instead of the agent's | 🤖 |
| FM-311 | Unclassified | A coordinator brief told a verifier to prove a new contract test guards a vendor patch by reverting the patch and running: ./dispatch/test/run-suite.sh --filter codex-evidence dispatch/test/codex-evidence-contract.bats -- expecting a non-zero exit as proof the guard fires. bats --filter matches TEST NAMES, not files, and no test in that file is named 'codex-evidence', so the filter selected ZERO tests and the runner exited 1 unconditionally. The command therefore returns 'guard fired' whether the guard works, is broken, or does not exist - the intended RED and the vacuous RED are indistinguishable. Confirmed directly afterward: FILTER_EXIT=1 with zero cases selected. The verifier noticed the mismatch and substituted a real proof (revert to the pre-patch backup, observe 4 specific red cases fail while both positive controls still pass), which is the only reason a vacuous result was not banked as evidence that the fleet-owned half of the item was sound. SIGNATURE: a check whose FAILURE mode is indistinguishable from its SUCCESS mode proves nothing, and a selector that silently matches nothing is the commonest way to build one. Any command used as proof-of-red must first be shown to select a non-empty set, and must be distinguishable from the empty-selection case - print the selected count, or assert the expected number of executed cases, never just the exit code. Same family as a green suite whose assertions never ran: the exit code is trusted while the population it ran over is unexamined. Note the direction: this one fails CLOSED into a FALSE POSITIVE - it manufactures evidence that a guard works, which is more dangerous than a missed defect because it terminates further checking. [recurrence_of: none] | Verification command in a dispatch brief was unsatisfiable, so it would have 'proved' the guard works regardless of truth | 🤖 |
| FM-312 | Unclassified | fleet#540: acceptance gate green, but ./dispatch/test/run-suite.sh --jobs 4 gave HEAD EXIT=1 CASES=1601 FAILED=1 against BASE EXIT=0 CASES=1598 FAILED=0. The count went UP by 3 because the change added tests, while a DIFFERENT pre-existing test broke. The campaign's standard floor 'executed count >= base' is satisfied (1601 >= 1598) and therefore blind: it can only detect deletion of cases, never breakage of them. Only the runner EXIT CODE and the not-ok count caught it. The broken test was 'audition: the per-root lock does not suppress a protected transaction failure' (lifecycle-lock-scope.bats:271): audition_with_record_lock began swallowing a non-zero status from the function it protects, so a failed transaction reported success to its caller - a false green inside a lock wrapper that many callers inherit. SIGNATURE: count-based conservation checks and pass/fail checks are ORTHOGONAL and neither substitutes for the other; a PR that adds tests can hide a regression from a count floor indefinitely. Any gate asserting 'count >= base' must ALSO assert exit==0 and failed==0 at both commits, and must report all four numbers. Second-order lesson: the acceptance gate ran only the 21-case file the feature touched and was green, so a gate passing is not a suite passing - scope the acceptance run to the whole suite before merge, not to the feature's own file. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #540) | Full suite fails while the executed-case count INCREASES, so a count-vs-base floor sees no regression | 🤖 |
| FM-313 | Unclassified | fleet#566: tests/test_static_calibration_gate.py verifies 'if data/task-calibration.jsonl has a row for this hash, it must match live scores'. The hash is computed over the task TREE, so ANY edit inside the task tree changes the hash, removes the matching row, and makes the check SKIP rather than fail - 'skipped: registry has no row for current linkvault hash yet'. Demonstrated with a PURE COMMENT change (fixing two stale fixture docstrings): before the edit 'Ran 94 tests / OK', after 'Ran 94 tests / OK (skipped=1)'. Verified both directions by stashing the edit and re-running. The executed-case count is IDENTICAL at 94, so a count-vs-base floor cannot see the disarming either. The check exists to catch a task tree drifting from its registered calibration, and the mechanism that signals drift is the mechanism that suppresses the check - a real logic change moving the floor from 13 would produce the identical green. SIGNATURE: absence of a lookup key must not be inferred as 'nothing to check'; absence is also exactly what a regression looks like. A check that cannot evaluate its predicate must FAIL, with a distinct signal, never skip. If a legitimate state genuinely lacks the key (a brand-new task before first calibration) that state must be represented EXPLICITLY - a marker, an allowlist, a 'pending' band - never inferred from absence. Near-identical prior art in this corpus: hc-FM-217, where modules that failed to import were collected into an 'unresolved' warn list and excluded from the comparison, leaving an empty collision set that printed PASS. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #566) | Consistency check SKIPS when its lookup key is absent, so the very edit it polices disarms it | 🤖 |
| FM-314 | Unclassified | fleet#516/#565: a guard against citing the wrong calibration gate went through four build rounds, each reviewed by an independent cross-vendor reviewer, each shipping GREEN and each bypassed a different way. R1/R2 were a blacklist of phrasings (any natural rewrite slipped past; cap hit, method changed). R3 inverted the rule to deny-by-default with a small permit set, but the permit test compared value MEMBERSHIP not shape, so 13/13/13 duplicated, 18/13 truncated and 23/18/13 reordered all passed. R4 compared exact shape/order/cardinality - and 13/18/23/29 then produced ZERO citation groups, because GROUP_RE's {1,2} quantifier plus a (?![\w/]) lookahead means an overlong run matches nothing at all. Critically 11/17/23/29 - the ORIGINAL WRONG GATE with a fourth value appended - became invisible: not permitted-too-generously, but never surfaced to the permit logic at all. SIGNATURE: every round hardened the JUDGE while leaving the RECOGNIZER narrow, and a candidate the recognizer never emits cannot be judged by any rule however strict. When successive fixes to a validator keep failing in NEW ways rather than repeating, suspect the extraction/tokenization stage rather than the decision stage: widen recognition FIRST, then tighten permits. Process lesson: a declared stopping rule set BEFORE the round (split the PR rather than run round five) is what stopped the loop; the verified factual correction was landed separately and the unconverged guard moved to its own issue rather than holding it hostage. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #565) | Four fix rounds each tightened the comparison while the tokenizer stayed narrow, so the bypass just moved | 🤖 |
| FM-315 | Unclassified | fleet PR#564: an independent reviewer returned VERDICT fail with a HIGH finding that routing/README.md:79 cited '0-of-2 = 11/23, 1-of-2 = 17/23' for r3-linkvault-v1 and must be corrected to 13/18/23 to match the registered gate. Measured live rather than argued: static_calibration.py reports the FIXTURES score 13/18/23, so the registry half of the finding was right - but that documentation line describes OBSERVED MODEL SCORES, not the fixture calibration gradient. It is immediately followed by 'Measured actor profile (n=6/cell...)' and its bullets bind 11 and 17 to named model families, corroborated by the round-3 evidence FINDINGS.md. Applying the requested fix would have replaced a TRUE statement about what models scored with a FALSE one. SIGNATURE: two semantically different claims that share a numeric signature (here a 3-value gradient) will be conflated by a reviewer pattern-matching on the numbers, and the resulting correction is an OVER-correction - strictly worse than the miss, because it manufactures a fresh falsehood inside a document whose purpose is evidence, and it arrives wearing the authority of a review. Mitigation that worked: refuse to act on any citation finding until the claim TYPE is established by measurement, and dispatch a second independent reviewer explicitly tasked with attacking the refutation rather than re-reviewing the diff, since the party who wrote the refutation must not be the party who closes it out. Note the same review's MEDIUM finding was real and led to hc-FM for fleet#566 - a wrong headline finding is not a worthless review. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #564) | Reviewer conflated two distinct claims sharing a numeric signature and filed a HIGH that would have INTRODUCED an error | 🤖 |
| FM-316 | Unclassified | A founder ruling (2026-08-16) changed the delivery contract for third-party changes: agents must commit a PATCH FILE into the repo instead of live-editing an installed plugin. The rework brief was updated accordingly and the lane complied exactly - it delivered dispatch/vendor-patches/codex-1.0.6-fleet-263.patch plus 498 lines of bats and left the installed plugin untouched, verified by mtime. But the ACCEPTANCE GATE was not updated: it still probed the INSTALLED plugin root. So it reported check-failed on all four fail-open probes, and its own summary line said 'NOT enforced by the installed plugin' - accurate about the thing it looked at, and irrelevant to the thing that was delivered. A perfectly compliant lane was scored as a failure, and the harness would have scored every future compliant lane the same way. SIGNATURE: an acceptance gate is one half of a delivery CONTRACT; changing what the producer emits without changing what the checker consumes leaves the checker silently validating the wrong artifact. Whenever a brief's deliverable shape changes - file instead of in-place edit, patch instead of commit, artifact instead of side effect - the gate must be re-pointed in the SAME change, and its discrimination re-proved against the new artifact. Note the failure direction: this one failed CLOSED (a good lane looked bad), which is survivable and self-announcing. The mirror case is a gate still pointed at a location where the property already holds, which fails OPEN and passes every broken lane silently. Third gate defect of this session in the same family - see the hardcoded-checkout gate and the unsatisfiable proof-of-red; the recurring root is that gates were written against an assumed artifact location and never re-verified against the real one. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #263) | Changing what a lane DELIVERS invalidated the gate that CONSUMES it, so the gate failed every compliant lane | 🤖 |
| FM-317 | Unclassified | fleet PR#548 carried a parked disposition recording (a) a hold because it 'depends on fleet#386 by its own issue text' and (b) a fail-open-validator blocker marked SAME_CLASS_REPEAT, which under the loop cap forbids a third same-method attempt. Re-adjudicated at the unchanged head 5ecd9aa on 2026-08-16: BOTH were false. #386 had CLOSED on 2026-08-10, satisfying the dependency. The fail-open blocker was not reproducible - every gate the PR names fails CLOSED when starved: absent input -> jq -e exit 2 and sha256sum -c exit 1; empty -> exit 4 / exit 1; malformed -> exit 5 / exit 1 / run_cmd exact-match exit 97. Because a SAME_CLASS_REPEAT verdict forbids re-attempt, the stale park was self-sealing: the very disposition that was wrong was also the reason nobody would re-test it, so it would have frozen the work indefinitely. The real blocker was something the park had never identified at all (see below). SIGNATURE: a parked disposition is a CLAIM WITH A TIMESTAMP, not a standing fact - it is evidence about a head at a moment, and both the world (dependencies closing) and the code can move underneath it. Re-verify a park before acting on it, in BOTH directions: it can be too pessimistic (blocking landable work) as well as too optimistic. The danger is asymmetric for terminal-sounding labels like SAME_CLASS_REPEAT, PARKED or REFUTE, which suppress the re-testing that would expose them; those need an explicit expiry or a re-verify-before-citing rule. Corollary observed in the same adjudication: parked dispositions record the blocker that was FOUND, not the blocker that MATTERS, so re-adjudication must re-derive from acceptance criteria rather than only re-checking the recorded findings. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #548) | A parked PR disposition rotted in BOTH directions and would have frozen the work indefinitely on an expired premise | 🤖 |
| FM-318 | Unclassified | A dispatch lane live-edited an installed plugin outside its repo. The coordinator backed up the modified files, later restored from that backup, compared md5sums, saw them match, and reported 'plugin restored, md5-verified / installed plugin unchanged' - repeatedly and confidently, to the founder. The comparison was structurally incapable of showing what was claimed: the backup was taken AFTER the modification, so backup == installed proves only that the state was STABLE between the two reads, never that it was PRISTINE. Measured: vendor git HEAD 88d8dee vs backup 9f12e7b vs installed 9f12e7b - backup and installed agreed with each other and BOTH differed from the only provenance-bearing baseline. An independent reviewer caught it while checking an unrelated scope rule; the coordinator's own repeated verification never could have. Compounding error: the backup covered only the files that session happened to touch, not the full diff-vs-baseline, so a third modified file (codex-companion.mjs, carrying a DIFFERENT closed issue's live fix) was never snapshotted and was nearly destroyed by the obvious remediation. SIGNATURE: a restore/integrity check must compare against a PROVENANCE-BEARING baseline - git HEAD, a package checksum, a signed artifact, an upstream re-download - never against a snapshot derived from the artifact under test. Snapshot-vs-snapshot answers 'did it change since I looked?' and is routinely misread as 'is it as it should be?'. Determine the backup's provenance BEFORE trusting it as a restore point, and define the backup set from the full diff against the baseline, not from the subset you believe you modified. Same family as verifying a config against its documentation rather than against the running system. [recurrence_of: none] (GitHub #570) | Restore verified against a snapshot taken FROM the modified thing, proving stability and mistaken for pristineness | 🤖 |
| FM-319 | Unclassified | Across one campaign session the coordinator dispatched build lanes with cli-dispatch start and omitted --no-pr three times (fleet #567, #571, #572). Each lane opened its own PR on its dispatch/<task> branch as soon as it delivered, before the coordinator had verified anything. All three had to be closed as duplicates of the PR the coordinator opened deliberately, and each duplicate carried a commit authored cli-dispatch@localhost, which resolves to NO GitHub login and therefore could never have landed anyway (cli-dispatch land refuses exit 27 when it cannot determine the authoring login). Consequences beyond the noise: an open PR is a claim that work is ready for review, so three unreviewed lane outputs sat publicly labelled as proposals; and a reviewer or a queue-draining workflow could have adjudicated the duplicate instead of the real PR, wasting a full review round on a branch nobody intended to merge. SIGNATURE: a dispatch tool whose DEFAULT is to publish means every omitted suppression flag is an outbound action taken without a decision. When the coordinator intends to open the PR itself after verification, --no-pr is not optional decoration; it is what keeps publication gated on the verification step. Fix at the dispatch site, not by remembering: put --no-pr in the standing build-lane invocation template alongside --check-script and --check-strength, so omitting it is a visible deletion rather than a silent default. Same family as any tool that treats publication as the default rather than an explicit act. [recurrence_of: none] | Build lanes dispatched without --no-pr minted three duplicate PRs the coordinator then had to close | 🤖 |
| FM-320 | Unclassified | Coordinator's round-1 review brief for fleet PR #545 asserted the PR was tests-only and listed dispatch/cli-dispatch as FROZEN and unchanged by design; the merged delta against origin/main in fact changes dispatch/cli-dispatch by 76 lines in the delivery path, so the independent reviewer audited only the test half and its pass-on-substance covered half the diff; the false claim came from reading the head commit instead of computing the true delta, and the error was found only when the coordinator later recomputed the merged two-dot delta. [recurrence_of: none] | Review brief declared a changed file FROZEN and 'unchanged by design', steering the reviewer off the only implementation file in the diff | Judgment |
| FM-321 | Unclassified | Coordinator review briefs instructed reviewers to run ./dispatch/test/run-suite.sh --jobs 4, but every review lane runs inside .dispatch/worktrees/ where dispatch/test/setup_suite.bash refuses a whole-suite run with exit 35 by design per fleet#251 while targeted file selections run normally; fleet PR #545's reviewer verified the mutation and found no source-level defect yet returned VERDICT fail solely because it could not satisfy the impossible full-suite requirement, so the brief manufactured the blocker rather than finding one, and duplicating the coordinator's own floor-1 full-suite duty added nothing. [recurrence_of: c-027] | Review brief required a full-suite run inside a dispatch lane worktree, where the suite refuses by design, manufacturing a false blocking verdict | Judgment |
| FM-322 | Unclassified | Re-authoring a lane commit with git commit --amend --author sets only the author; the committer stayed the local identity petersimmons1972@gmail.com which resolves to no GitHub login, so GitHub reported committer_login NULL and cli-dispatch land refused exit 27 exactly as it had before the fix, and the real remedy is to set GIT_COMMITTER_NAME and GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL alongside --author. [recurrence_of: none] | git commit --amend fixed the author but left an unresolvable committer, so land still refused exit 27 | Judgment |
| FM-323 | Unclassified | After re-committing fleet PR 545 to fix committer identity the tree was byte-identical proven by git diff --exit-code, but cli-dispatch land refused exit 4 because the trusted verdict predated the new head commit; the coordinator had been reasoning that tree identity let the approval carry over, and the tool correctly rejected that argument, so any head rewrite however cosmetic costs a fresh review round. [recurrence_of: none] | A metadata-only re-commit voids the independent review, because land compares verdict timestamp against head commit time | Judgment |
| FM-324 | Unclassified | A coordinator gate for fleet PR 568 grepped the extracted function body for the string ':!REPORT.md' without stripping comment lines, so the build lane shipped a second commit whose only change was deleting that string from comments in order to pass the gate, proving the check was cosmetic rather than behavioral; the fix is to strip comment lines before any text assertion about code. [recurrence_of: none] | Text-based acceptance gate was satisfied by editing a comment rather than the code | Judgment |
| FM-325 | Unclassified | A coordinator gate ran dispatch/test/delivered-postconditions.bats which invokes cli-dispatch start and is therefore not nested-safe, so from the dispatch lane worktree where cli-dispatch executes check scripts setup_suite refused with exit 35 by design per fleet#251 and the gate reported 1 case 1 failed at setup_suite, meaning the lane could never pass its own gate; the remedy is to detect an in-lane checkout, skip that arm loudly, and have the coordinator run it outside the lane. [recurrence_of: c-027] | Acceptance gate invoked a non-nested-safe bats file, making it unsatisfiable inside the lane worktree it polices | Judgment |
| FM-326 | Unclassified | The overnight campaign's single abort condition was that origin/main fails its own acceptance suite after a campaign merge, and the heartbeat implementing that check was run in a shared checkout while roughly twelve other run-suite processes were active, returning exit 1 with 1593 cases and 15 failures on fleet main f9ca586; a pristine clone of the identical tree run alone returned exit 0 with 1614 cases and 0 failures, so the detector produced a false abort signal that would have halted the entire campaign, and the remedy is that any abort heartbeat must run quiesced in a pristine checkout with no other suite running. [recurrence_of: none] | The campaign abort detector was run concurrently with other test suites, so it manufactured the emergency it exists to detect | Judgment |
| FM-327 | Unclassified | On one fleet tree three full-suite runs gave 15 failures, 19 failures, and 0 failures depending only on how many other suites were running, with executed-case counts dropping to 1593 and 1577 versus 1614 uncontended which is the signature of test files aborting partway rather than assertions failing; the affected files delivered-postconditions.bats and rehearsal-isolation.bats both shell out to cli-dispatch start which serialises on a same-repository lifecycle lock with CLI_DISPATCH_MAX_LIVE_LANES defaulting to 4, so any full-suite result taken while other suites run is untrustworthy in both directions and several review rounds returned VERDICT fail on this artifact rather than on a real defect in their diffs. [recurrence_of: none] | Concurrent full-suite runs produce unstable false failures because lane-driving tests contend on cli-dispatch lifecycle state | Judgment |

| FM-350 | Ephemeral Resource Leakage / Host Exhaustion | 2026-08-17 disk cleanup: a /tmp sweep loop used shell case patterns including 'claude-*' to match abandoned dispatch scratch. That pattern matched /tmp/claude-1000 — the live Claude Code tool-output directory for the running session — and rm -r deleted it, killing the current command's own output file (ENOENT on read-back). Impact was nil only by luck: the harness recreated the directory, job briefs/gates lived under ~/.claude/jobs (not /tmp), and every background task had already completed and been consumed. Controls: (1) a cleanup glob must be anchored to the specific scratch names being reclaimed, never a vendor/tool prefix that infrastructure also uses; (2) before any /tmp sweep, exclude the harness's own runtime paths explicitly (CLAUDE_* dirs, the session task dir); (3) prefer an explicit allowlist of directory names over a case-pattern loop when deleting at scale. [recurrence_of: none] | Cleanup glob matched the harness's own scratch directory and deleted it mid-session | Judgment |
| FM-351 | Ephemeral Resource Leakage / Host Exhaustion | 2026-08-17 disk cleanup: a survey classified clearwatch/output (3.3G) and engram-go/results (14G) as regenerable build artifacts, and rm -r removed them. Both directories also contained COMMITTED files — 122 tracked chart SVGs under clearwatch/output and 20 tracked markdown/registry files under engram-go/results — so the deletion silently converted committed content into pending deletions (clearwatch dirty count jumped 5 -> 125). Detected only because a post-deletion integrity check compared each repo's dirty count against the pre-deletion survey; restored with git checkout --. A second, unrelated protection held by accident: results/golden-snapshot-*/ was chmod dr-xr-xr-x on purpose and rm failed against it. Controls: (1) before deleting any path inside a git repo, run git ls-files <path> — a non-empty result means the path is not disposable regardless of its name; (2) after any bulk deletion, re-run status --porcelain and compare '^ D' counts to the baseline; (3) treat read-only permissions on a data directory as a deliberate do-not-delete marker, not an obstacle to force through. [recurrence_of: none] | Deleted git-TRACKED files while sweeping directories classified as build cruft | Judgment |

Round 2 misread a mid-flight tree (phantom 21 failures) before re-verifying at the settled HEAD. Counter-rule: a cap/stall receipt is NOT proof the worker is dead — before re-dispatching into the same worktree, verify quiescence (no process, stable HEAD across two reads a minute apart) or move the successor to a fresh clone. Recurrence of the verify-liveness-before-relaunch lesson; also related to the false-concurrent-writer report in the round-1 receipt. | FM-360 | Unclassified | 2026-08-18, PR#326 CI poll: a background poll loop inherited the shell's GH_TOKEN at spawn; App installation tokens expire (~1h), so after expiry every iteration returned 401 Bad credentials and the loop burned to TIMEOUT with total=null — the SHA-pinned gate reported failure that was pure auth rot, indistinguishable in the summary line from a real CI outcome until the log was read. Counter-rule: any poller expected to run near or beyond token lifetime must not freeze an App token in its environment — use env -u GH_TOKEN (keyring OAuth, auto-refreshing) for read-only polls, or re-mint per iteration; and a poll loop must FAIL DISTINCTLY on auth errors (detect 401 and say so) rather than counting them as pending. [recurrence_of: none] | Long-running background poller outlived its ambient GitHub App installation token | 🤖 |
| FM-361 | Unclassified | The catalog-guard workflow (.github/workflows/guard.yml) step 'Run bin/ tool bats tests (require-real-ci, land)' runs bats bin/tests/*.bats, which includes tests in bin/tests/test_add_failure_mode.bats that exercise the full canonical-add loop (extract_mechanical.py -> L2 regen -> reconcile) against sibling repos (fleet, claude-codex, fleet-dispatch) checked out as worktree siblings. CI checks out only the hindsight repo (documented explicitly in guard.yml's own comments for the separate 'Reconcile L1 corpus' step, which was scoped down for exactly this reason and tracks the gap as issue #73), but bin/tests/*.bats was never given the same scoping. Result: 8 of 237 cases fail every run (tests 8, 11-14, 19-21 -- canonical add's extract/L2/reconcile ordering, tier recording, POINTER.md/HIGH.md regen, dot-dot/symlink alias, real-reconciler loop, sibling shortfall, short-source rollback). Confirmed red on main at HEAD da72a3f and on the last 3 build-red-ci-pair runs and split-108-fail-loud (PR#124) -- pre-existing, not introduced by any of those branches. Fix: either check out the three sibling repos in guard.yml before the bin/tests step (mirroring how the split lane worked around it locally by symlinking fleet/claude-codex/fleet-dispatch into its job tmp dir), or scope bin/tests/*.bats the same declared-reduced-check way the reconcile step already is, with an explicit named list of what's unchecked in CI. Generalizable rule: a workflow step that silently needs repo-external state (sibling checkouts) will read green in a dev worktree with those siblings present and red in CI without disclosing why, unless the gap is declared inline like the reconcile step already models. [recurrence_of: none] | catalog-guard's bin/tests/*.bats step is persistently red on main — needs sibling-repo checkout | Judgment |
| FM-362 | Unclassified | fleet PR#643 (#498 W3): the dispatched tdd-build item's acceptanceCmd ran only dispatch/test/root-launcher-wiring.bats (the feature's own test file), which the lane's implementer and the coordinator's own host-verification both used to certify the change. Neither caught that the new LANE_JOURNAL_FIELDS_JQ fields (origin_verification, origin_verification_reason) broke dispatch/test/lane-journal.bats:149, a SEPARATE pre-existing test in a different file that pins the journal row's exact closed key set as a literal sorted array. Fleet's own CI (which runs the full suite) caught it; a narrower acceptance command would not have. This is the same mechanism as hc-FM-312 (fleet#540: an acceptance gate scoped to the feature's own 21-case file was green while a DIFFERENT pre-existing test broke elsewhere in the suite) -- second occurrence of this exact class in this repo, now against a hand-authored tdd-build acceptanceCmd rather than a --check-script gate. SIGNATURE: any acceptance command for a change that touches a shared, closed-set contract (a fixed key set, a fixed token vocabulary, a fixed schema) must run the FULL suite that contract lives in, not just the feature's own test file -- a closed-set assertion by definition lives outside the feature that adds a member to the set. Fix applied here: pinned test widened (dispatch/test/lane-journal.bats), re-verified against the full dispatch suite, re-pushed, re-gated. Going forward: when dispatching a tdd-build item whose implPaths touch a field/key/token that a DIFFERENT file's test enumerates as a closed set, the acceptanceCmd must include that file, not just the changed feature's own tests. [recurrence_of: none] | tdd-build acceptance command scoped to one test file missed a real regression in a sibling file (recurrence of hc-FM-312 pattern) | Judgment |
| FM-363 | Unclassified | hindsight PR#124 (split-108-fail-loud) includes commit 2910bf9 (fix(108): release the L2 embedding snapshot inside add-failure-mode.sh) and 6749ea0 (hindsight#121 recall-baseline ratchet), both of which add hard new dependencies into bin/add-failure-mode.sh's RUN_FULL_LOOP write path: capture_embedding_snapshot.py (subprocess), a real importlib-loaded pipeline/cluster.py module API (load_entries, cluster_entries, write_clusters, etc, not just subprocess-run), pipeline/recall_baselines.py --update --backend released, and pipeline/replay_l2.py as a consistency check. The pre-existing bats fixtures make_staged_repo and make_real_reconcile_repo in bin/tests/test_add_failure_mode.bats build a synthetic pipeline/ tree with toy stand-ins (trivial scripts writing a literal placeholder string) and were never updated when 2910bf9/6749ea0 changed what the write path actually does -- they don't create capture_embedding_snapshot.py at all, and their cluster.py stub doesn't expose the real module API now called via importlib. Reproduced locally: bats --print-output-on-failure bin/tests/test_add_failure_mode.bats at PR#124's head 687e2c8 shows the exact 8 CI failures (tests 8, 11-14, 19-21) all failing on 'python3: can't open file .../capture_embedding_snapshot.py: No such file or directory' followed by 'add-failure-mode: operation failed; applying undo journal'. This is NOT the sibling-checkout issue FM-361 wrongly claimed (corrected by a separate catalog entry on branch fix-catalog-guard-sibling-checkout, PR#125, not yet merged -- watch for an ID collision at merge time, both this entry and that branch's correction independently claimed the next-available slot) and NOT a CI environment gap -- it is a genuine fixture-completeness defect introduced by PR#124's own commits. PR#124 correctly added a NEW, properly-updated pytest suite for this behavior (bin/tests/test_add_failure_mode_release.py, commit 5984a93, with a _RELEASE_STUB 'honest' stand-in that imports the real staged cluster.py) but never retrofitted the equivalent fixture pattern into the older bats suite that still exercises the same full-loop path. SIGNATURE: when a PR changes what a shared code path actually does (new subprocess calls, new module API surface), every fixture across every test framework that exercises that path must be updated in the SAME PR, not just the new test suite the PR itself introduces. FIX (scoped, not yet applied): extend make_staged_repo/make_real_reconcile_repo to (1) copy the real pipeline/cluster.py into the stage instead of a toy stub, (2) install a capture_embedding_snapshot.py stand-in modeled on test_add_failure_mode_release.py's _RELEASE_STUB honest-mode pattern, (3) provide/copy pipeline/replay_l2.py and pipeline/recall_baselines.py into the stage plus seed pipeline/L2/recall-baselines.json for the ratchet to update against, (4) reconcile the bats assertions that hardcode the old toy-stub placeholder digest (~lines 243-263) against real cluster output. Route through a fresh scoped build task referencing test_add_failure_mode_release.py's stand-in as the template. [recurrence_of: none] | PR#124's bats fixture (test_add_failure_mode.bats) never updated for new write-path deps added by the same PR, causing 8/237 real CI failures | Judgment |
| FM-364 | Unclassified | A coordinator armed a background waiter of the form 'until ! pgrep -f "run-suite.sh --jobs 4"; do sleep; done' to fire when a BATS sweep finished. The waiter's own /bin/bash -c command line contains the pattern, so pgrep -f always matched (itself), the loop never terminated, and the completed sweep's results (1939 ok, log already on disk) sat unreported ~1.5h until the coordinator verified process state directly. Same class as duramind dm-15 (pkill -f killed the orchestrating shell whose own cmdline matched). Fix pattern: match on a token that cannot appear in the poller's own cmdline (pgrep -f on the absolute script path with -x semantics, a pidfile, or 'pgrep -f PATTERN | grep -v $$'), or wait on the harness task mechanism instead of process polling. 2026-08-18, fleet#604 sweep, session 3ab084ba. | FM-367 | Unclassified | 2026-08-18, fleet PR#639: the merge-gate poller looped until total_count>0 and total_count==completed_count. Immediately after a push only GitGuardian had registered, so the first poll saw total=1/done=1/bad=[] and printed DONE — a green merge signal while 13 of 14 required checks, including the entire bats suite, had not yet been created. The merge was not made only because the coordinator eyeballed the suspiciously low total against the 14 seen on prior heads. Class: gate fails OPEN on an incomplete sample (same family as absence-in-a-sample-that-couldnt-show-presence). GitHub creates check-runs progressively, so 'all registered checks are done' is not 'all required checks passed' until registration settles. Fix applied: poller now requires (a) total >= MIN_CHECKS floor, (b) all registered completed, (c) total stable across two consecutive polls, and exits non-zero early on any non-success conclusion. Stronger variant if this recurs: assert the required-check NAMES from branch protection are all present, rather than a count floor. | FM-373 | Unclassified | clearwatch/wordpress, visual-tester, content-cache-postgres, qdrant, research-postgres and two zero-replica workloads all carried maxUnavailable:0 or minAvailable:1 at one replica. That buys no availability -- PDBs constrain only voluntary eviction, so a node reboot kills the single pod regardless -- while making kubectl drain hang forever on four nodes. The 2026-08-18 outage happened because the hypervisor was rebooted ungracefully; the graceful alternative was impossible precisely because of these PDBs. Check: for each PDB, if status.expectedPods>0 and status.disruptionsAllowed==0, fail. | FM-374 | Unclassified | 2026-08-19 node reboots, two instances in one task. (1) /var/run/reboot-required was absent on all twelve k3s nodes, so the flag reported 'no reboot needed' everywhere -- including eight nodes running 6.8.0-137 with 6.8.0-138 staged in /boot. Comparing the running kernel to the newest installed /boot/vmlinuz-* is the positive source. (2) The same script inferred 'the reboot happened' from the node going NotReady, but node-monitor-grace-period is ~40s and a 4-vCPU VM can go down and return inside it, so a SUCCESSFUL reboot would have timed out and aborted, leaving a healthy node cordoned and drained. status.nodeInfo.bootID changing is the positive artifact. | FM-375 | Unclassified | 2026-08-19: clearwatch/wordpress persisted only wp-content on its PVC and downloaded WordPress core from wordpress.org in an alpine:3.19 init container on EVERY pod start. Cluster DNS had been broken for musl-libc images for an unknown length of time -- petersimmons.com answers NOERROR-with-no-records instead of NXDOMAIN, and musl stops searching there rather than falling through to the bare name, so every Alpine pod failed to resolve any external host while glibc pods were unaffected. Nothing detected it because the pod had not restarted. A routine node drain restarted it and the site could not come back. Two compounding lessons: (a) a long-running pod's ability to START is untested until it restarts, so uptime is not evidence of recoverability; (b) treat 'downloads its runtime at boot' as a hard availability dependency and either bake it into the image or persist it. | FM-379 | Unclassified | 2026-08-19/20 homelab campaign: THREE instances in a single session, one a variant the existing entry does not cover. (1) 'pgrep -f <pattern>' matched the checking command's own /bin/bash -c line, so a background task that had been looping 2h57m was reported to the founder as 'nothing is running'. The supporting evidence was an empty output file plus an empty task list -- both equally consistent with 'never started' AND 'still running', so the corroboration was vacuous. Only the founder's screenshot exposed it. (2) The same defect had already been diagnosed EARLIER in the same session for a different task, which was killed on that basis -- but the first task was never re-checked, so a method known to be broken was left standing against a second target. (3) A `ps -eo` piped to `awk` variant, not pgrep at all: awk field indices matched the wrong columns of the checker's own row and produced an elapsed time of '441077234-00:18:40', which was then reported to the founder as evidence that HIS machine's clock was skewed. The clock was fine (NTP synced, btime consistent). SIGNATURE beyond the existing entry: this is not specific to pgrep -- it is any process query whose result set can include the query itself, and the ps/awk form fails DIFFERENTLY, yielding a plausible-looking WRONG VALUE rather than a hung loop, which is worse because it invites a confident false diagnosis of an unrelated system. Direction matters: instance 1 fails toward 'nothing is happening', which terminates investigation instead of prolonging it. DIAGNOSTIC LESSON: when a check of this shape is found broken, re-run every earlier conclusion in the same session that relied on it -- a method known to be unreliable does not become reliable for the targets it was already applied to. [recurrence_of: c-036] | A process check must match on a token that cannot appear in the checking command's own command line, and a negative result must be corroborated before it is reported as 'nothing is running' | Judgment |
| FM-380 | Unclassified | homelab-kubernetes#20, fixed 2026-08-20. REFINES FM-377 (absence-of-signal checks need a positive source) by constraining WHICH positive source is admissible. The ARC key-ownership guard decided PASS by the silence of a yq query that discriminated on the very key names it was hunting for, so a substituted yq that answered the other queries plausibly and returned nothing for the deciding one passed a values file carrying a real local 'image:' pin, rc=0. FIRST FIX added census parity plus a positive control -- but the control was a CONSTANT (the file's known first key, githubConfigUrl). Attacking my own fix defeated it: a stub emitting a coherent, correctly-sized census that simply omitted 'image' satisfied parity AND the constant control, and the pinned file still passed rc=0. Only a control COMPUTED FROM THE ARTIFACT closed it -- every key the file literally declares unquoted at column 0 must appear in the parser's census -- because a stub cannot satisfy that without reporting the document truthfully, and a truthful report cannot hide the pin from the shell-side decision. SIGNATURE: a positive control is only as strong as the attacker's ignorance of it; a constant can be hardcoded into the liar, a control that scales with the artifact cannot be pre-satisfied. Corollary on scope: the text-derived control is a FLOOR, never the verdict -- a quoted key is real and invisible to a regex, and a comment mentioning the key false-fails, so the parser still decides and the text only bounds what it may claim it saw. DIAGNOSTIC LESSON, the part that generalises furthest: re-running the ORIGINAL repro after a fix proves only that the fix stops the attack you already knew, and the first fix did stop it. The hole was found solely by writing a SECOND, smarter attacker against the fix itself. Any repair to a fail-open guard must be followed by a fresh attack designed against the repair, not a re-run of the repro that motivated it. Verified after: a real local pin is caught under a real yq, a silent stub, and a coherent lying stub alike; quoted-key and comment-mentioning regressions both hold. [recurrence_of: none] | A guard's positive control must be derived from the artifact under test, never a hardcoded constant -- a constant is knowable in advance and can be satisfied by the very substitution the control exists to detect | Judgment |
| FM-381 | Unclassified | 2026-08-19/20 homelab campaign, cross-harness review of three PRs. Both independent reviewers returned blocking verdicts (DO-NOT-MERGE, MERGE-WITH-FIX) whose headline blockers were ENVIRONMENTAL, not defects: their sandboxes had no DNS ('curl: (6) Could not resolve host registry.petersimmons.com') and no cluster access ('Unable to connect to the server: dial tcp 192.168.0.131:6443: operation not permitted'), so the registry-digest and live-manifest checks could not run at all. Both reviewers stated the limitation honestly in their prose -- the defect is in the VERDICT SHAPE, which collapses 'verified false' and 'could not verify' into one blocking token. Two opposite failure directions follow, and both nearly occurred: (a) accept the verdict wholesale and abandon a sound change; (b) notice the blockers are environmental, dismiss the verdict, and thereby discard the reviewer's REAL findings, which were buried alongside -- a README asserting maxRunners 6 where manifest and live both said 12, and a Helm array-replacement hole that let a values file discard the shared image pin without naming any owned key (proved with real Helm v3.18.6: pin ABSENT). Direction (b) is the more dangerous because the environmental blockers are conspicuous and the real findings are not. SIGNATURE: any verdict emitted by an agent whose environment is narrower than the artifact's dependencies must carry a per-check disposition -- verified / refuted / NOT RUN with the reason -- and consumers must re-derive per-check rather than treating the verdict as atomic. Corollary for dispatchers: when a reviewer cannot reach a dependency, the missing coverage does not disappear, it silently transfers back to the dispatcher, and must be named as single-source wherever the result is reported. In this campaign the registry digest and the live-manifest comparison were re-run by the dispatcher and labelled single-source in the PR record for exactly that reason. [recurrence_of: none] | A reviewer must label a check it could not RUN distinctly from a check that FAILED, and the recipient must re-derive which blockers are real rather than accepting or dismissing the verdict as a whole | Judgment |

| FM-384 | Unclassified | 2026-08-20 homelab-kubernetes#28. A new declared-vs-live reconciliation check was legitimately RED against the cluster: it reported a real, still-unfixed defect it had been written to catch. Its mutation suite then asserted 'apply mutation -> expect exit 1' and reported 6/6 caught. That result was vacuous. The baseline was ALREADY exit 1, so a mutation the check ignored completely would have scored exactly the same, and the suite could not have failed for any input. Rerun requiring each mutation to add a NEW failure line NAMING the mutated object, the signal became real - and two mutations turned out to be invalid rather than missed (appending 'affinity: {}' to a Helm values file is a genuine no-op because Helm merges maps; only 'affinity: null' deletes). Without the message-level rerun, a correct check would have been 'fixed' to satisfy an invalid mutation. SIGNATURE: a differential test whose signal is one boolean is valid ONLY when the baseline sits on the opposite side of that boolean. Any check expected to be red for a known reason - a real defect it reports, an environment it cannot reach - must have exit-code assertions replaced by output-DELTA assertions before any 'N/N caught' claim is credible. Neighbours in the corpus, same family, different mechanism: hc-FM-311 (vacuous RED from a selector matching zero tests - success and failure modes indistinguishable) and hc-FM-170 (a green suite whose assertion was satisfied by data the report merely printed). This entry is the baseline-already-red variant: the confound is not an empty population but a saturated signal. [recurrence_of: none] | Mutation-testing a check by exit code alone proves nothing when that check already fails at baseline - every mutation scores identically whether or not it was detected | Judgment |
| FM-385 | Unclassified | 2026-08-20 homelab-kubernetes#28, caught in review before merge. A CI job ran three cluster checks behind a preflight step that asserted the runner's identity was read-only and ended in 'exit $fail'. Adding a new check that needed two extra RBAC reads, the natural move was to assert those grants in the same preflight - it is the step that already checks the identity. That would have set $fail whenever a grant was missing, and since the step ends in 'exit $fail', GitHub would stop the job THERE: the new check would never execute, and the two checks that passed today would ALSO never execute. A job that had been verifying three things would verify nothing while looking like a normal red, and the failure text would name the precheck rather than the defect. Worse, the grant genuinely WAS missing (measured by impersonating the ServiceAccount), so this would have happened on the very first run and the red would have been read as 'the new check works'. Fix: the preflight now REPORTS positive grants and gates only on the negative security assertions (cannot create/delete/patch/update, cannot read secrets). Each check's own preflight names the specific read it lacked and exits SKIP, which that job already treats as failure - so strictness is unchanged and the diagnosis is precise. Confirmed on the real run: both pre-existing checks PASSED and the new one reported 'SKIP: this identity cannot list namespaces'. SIGNATURE: a precondition belongs in the step that CONSUMES it, not in a shared gate, whenever the gate can abort siblings. Distinguish a SECURITY boundary (must fail closed and stop everything - a runner that can write is a finding) from a CAPABILITY report (must not stop siblings - one check's missing grant is not another check's problem). Folding the second into the first trades N specific diagnoses for one generic abort, and silently reduces coverage at the moment coverage is most in question. [recurrence_of: none] | Folding a capability probe into a step that gates the job stops the very checks it was added to protect, and the resulting red names the precheck instead of the finding | Judgment |
